# UI — the one design record

Everything known about Avra's UI in one file: the design as it stands, the work
that never landed, and every idea from the nine design docs this file replaces.
Assembled 2026-11-16 against branch `program`.

## How to read this

- **The design as built** is short and comes first.
- **Unlanded work** lists every branch-held idea that `program` lacks.
- **The idea ledger** holds all 1061 ideas of the nine source docs, grouped by
  topic, then by status. Each entry names its source doc and line range.
- A status is a reading of `program` on 2026-11-16, by grep, not by a build.

Status words: `PLANNED` not built · `PARTIAL` some of it stands · `BUILT` ·
`OVERRIDDEN` a later decision went another way · `REJECTED` turned down, with
the reason · `UNKNOWN` not cheaply checkable · `OTHER` framing and prior art.

## The design as built

- **Packages:** `packages/std-ui` (the library), `std-ui_http` (serving),
  `std-ui_gallery`; `tools/ui-board` is the proving program; `runtime/dom`
  is the page host.
- **A program mounts a view** with `mount(view)`. This replaced `app` /
  `view` / `ViewCx`.
- **A component is the node.** A `state` is a Cell with identity; it stands in
  a component instance, at module level, in a fn parameter or a record field.
  A child component's head takes no `state` seat, so values are threaded down
  and events back up by hand.
- **The diff is written in Avra.** It replaced the realizer visitor. Targets
  with no differ of their own (DOM, canvas, print) take an op stream over a
  binary, versioned wire (version 15).
- **Handlers are closures**, spelled `on press`. The generated-`Msg`,
  tree-as-data design lost.
- **`provide` / `env` are gone and nothing replaced them:** no `ambient` seat
  exists in the code. A theme is set once, at `mount`; no subtree overrides it.
- **Layout is a native solver** over a flex subset, not Yoga. There is no grid.
- **The web target is wasm32** with tasks running in the browser (JSPI by
  default, Asyncify as the fallback); pages hydrate.
- **Exact patches:** a page watches the cells its texts read and patches only
  those. Off unless `AVRA_EXACT` is set.
- **Assets** state their kind in the declaration: `asset logo: Picture = …`.
- **Data:** `@model` and `@crud` derive doors; observations are cached
  (slices 1–4), a router announces what changed, and typed routes carry
  `ask` / `send` (slices 1–2).
- **`within`** is a core keyword.
- **Theming is partly wired:** `Style` declares `elevation`, `text_size`,
  `line_height` and `tracking` and no target emits them; colour roles are a
  closed list of seven; a mounted page does not follow the OS dark mode; the
  terminal ignores the theme.
- **An event names no target:** a component declares `on press`; the page's
  table (`realize/html/delivery.av`) and the terminal's (`terminal/delivery.av`)
  each hold one row per primitive and event, typed against `DomEvent` and `Key`.
  A seat a target's table does not name is refused as the program builds.
- **Targets:** `html`, `dom` and the terminal. No native realizer exists.

## Direction

Owner decisions of 2026-11-17.

- **The aim:** a UI library better than any that exists: powerful, declarative, beautiful, easy,
  portable, fast. Consequential decisions are studied (prior art and what hurt in it, the hard
  cases as real programs, candidate designs, a spike where paper cannot decide), never made in
  passing.
- **The finish line** is one excellent cross-platform app, reached by milestones: the web first,
  then iOS.
- **The order:** component authoring, theming and state, proven on the web with a small set of
  components; then the same small set on iOS, before any other path goes far. "Parity with Swift"
  means our iOS target does what our web target does, not SwiftUI's whole surface.
- **Five foundations decide the rest:** state and sync; how a native target draws; the authoring
  surface; the update model (exact patches on every target, or not); distribution to devices.
- **Sync is first class.** State is designed with local, remote and peer-to-peer data on the table
  from the start, so that an app is written barely thinking about syncing; presence falls out of
  it. Where a rule cannot survive a merge (a unique name, a balance that stays positive, "only the
  owner deletes") something arbitrates, and the design says where that line is. Design text exists
  (`docs/2026_10_04_SHARING_MODEL.md`, `docs/2026_09_24_AUTONOMOUS_SYSTEMS_AND_STATE.md`); no code.
- **A library before syntax.** A want is first a component, an annotation, a `collect` or a derive;
  then one small general feature that makes it a library matter; and only then new syntax, in the
  most current, declarative spelling the language has.
- **Out of the first finish line, kept in the ledger:** generative UI; Android (after iOS);
  products and paywalls (a library matter, later). Deploy is not now, and a way onto every device is
  owed.

Studied and decided in outline: authoring, context, theming, and how a native target draws
(leading, to be proved by a spike), all below. Owed after the first milestone: animation (ticket
avra-8sb5.59.30.1.32); state and sync; distribution.

**The first milestone: the board, on the web, written the way this record says.** It is done when
`tools/ui-board` uses a `state` head seat, a default slot and named blocks, a key that is its id,
`ctx theme`, the Crisp look as theme data, and its claims hold. No further study opens before it.

An earlier ten-stage outline of the 652 planned or partly built ideas (finish what stands; screens
and navigation; core components; data on the device; actions and forms; theme, accessibility and
motion; assets; dev tooling; more targets; agents and security) is the ledger's content by stage;
the studies above set the order.

## Authoring: decisions

Owner decisions of 2026-11-17, from the component authoring study. A component here is any
component (a queue, a command, a server), not only a UI one.

- **A component's own names are read through `self.`**, as today: head props, fields and
  state are `self.x`, and what stands outside the component is bare, so a reader tells the
  two apart at sight. Rejected: all bare (decided and withdrawn the same day; it hides which
  names are the component's); a leading dot, `.x` (it is the spelling of an enum variant,
  `shows: .All`).
- **A place is passed with no marker.** A seat declared `state` takes the caller's place:
  `stepper count`, `field "Name" { value: draft }`. Passing what is no place is a compile
  error at the call. Rejected: a call-site sigil (SwiftUI's `$x`, Svelte's `bind:`), since
  `state` fn parameters already take `stepper(c)` bare.
- **`state` stands in a component head**, as it does in a fn's parameters.
- **`state` always means a place** (owner 2026-11-18). A place is a `state` binding (local, module
  or seat) or a `state` field by any path (`self.draft`, `t.title`); a `state` field holds a Cell a
  copy of its record shares. A member `state x: T = d` given a place shares it; given nothing, the
  instance keeps its own. A value passed to `state` is a compile error, with no exception for a
  literal. A seed is an ordinary input the member's default reads once:

  ```avra
  component counter(start: int = 0) { state n: int = self.start }
  counter { start: 10 }     // seeded
  counter { n: count }      // shared
  counter { n: 10 }         // refused
  ```

  Rejected: a value seeds and a place shares under one spelling (`field { value: t.title }` over a
  plain field compiles and never saves); the same with a guard that refuses a changeable name (a
  rule the reader cannot see). Unbuilt: a member default reading a prop. Not solved here, for the
  state-and-sync study: a place through a `state` list of plain records (`todos[i].title`), and a
  place made of a read and a write.
- **A control's value is a place** (ruled 2026-11-18, built 2026-11-19). `field`, `checkbox`,
  `switch` and `dialog` hold `state value`, `state checked`, `state open`: given a place they share
  it (`field "Name" { value: draft }`), given none they keep their own, seeded from `initial`, which
  stands because the place's default reads it (`state value: string = self.initial`); a value passed
  there is refused as any `state` seat refuses one. Every control whose gesture writes a value has
  this one shape (later `select`, `slider`, `radio`). The primitive derive reads two marks for it
  (UI lead, 2026-11-19), and refuses either on a component that is no primitive:
  - `@sets(place)` on an event writes what it says into the place before the caller's handler, on
    every target, so a handlerless `field` keeps what is typed; its one payload is the place's type.
    `@sets(place, constant)` writes a constant that fits the place (`@sets(open, false) on dismiss`).
    Several events may set one place, each marked.
  - `@over(place)` on `shown: T?` (the place is `state … : T`, at most one per place): the node
    shows `shown ?? place`, told each paint and never written, so every target, the DOM page
    included, is held to what the program shows after a gesture. The mark's own law: an instance
    handing a place for `place` and `shown` both is refused, naming both, and told which to drop.
- **A slot is a field of views, and one slot may be the default.** A composition's field of
  type `List<dyn View>` is a slot: no keyword, no slot-only mark. `@default`, the mark that
  already names a component's default event, may stand on one slot instead; a component has
  one default, an event or a slot, and a block's bare content fills it. Every other slot is
  filled by a block under its name. Rejected: a `slot` keyword and `@children` on a
  composition (a second spelling of what a typed field already says); "the first slot is the
  default" (a rule the reader cannot see).

  ```avra
  component panel(title: string) {
      @default body: List<dyn View> = []
      actions: List<dyn View> = []
      column {
          heading self.title
          self.body
          row { self.actions }
      }
  }

  panel "Settings" {
      toggle dense
      actions { button "Done" {} }
  }
  ```

  Unbuilt: `@default` on a field is refused ("only an event can be a component's default"),
  and a slot takes `actions: collect { … }`, not `actions { … }`.
- **A key is any comparable value**: `key: t.id`, not `key: "${t.id}"`.
- **`words` and `reads` leave every primitive**: the derive that needs them adds them.
- **An event names no target.** A component declares `on press`; each target's own table maps
  it to a click, a key or a tap (ruled 2026-10-18).
- **The word is `ctx`**, not `ambient`, for a value that reaches everything inside a
  block without being passed. No prop drilling is the requirement; see Context below.
- **No lifecycle hook stands in for a missing mechanism.** What a hook is used for elsewhere has a
  home here: fetching when shown is an observation; a timer or a subscription is a task the instance
  owns, cancelled when it leaves; focusing a field is state; animating in and out is declared on the
  node, since the diff knows what entered and left; "screen viewed" is a real `on appear` event;
  reaching into the platform is an escape hatch.
- **Animation is its own study** (enter and exit, a value changing, a keyed move, following
  a finger; the last wants the frame scheduler).
- **Native by default.** A target draws with the platform's own controls and looks native
  out of the box; an author may theme. The web carries a default theme of its own. Drawing
  every pixel ourselves is ruled out.

Probed on `program`, 2026-11-17 (`build/avra-try`):

| Form | Answer |
|---|---|
| `fn f(state n: int)` | works (`tests/param_state`) |
| `type R = { state xs: List<int> = [] }` | works (`tests/field_state`) |
| `component c(state n: int)` | `parse.expected`: expected `{` |
| `panel "x" { text "a" }` over `body: List<dyn View>`, placed by `self.body` | works, in written order |
| `body: [...]` and `actions: [...]` over two such fields | works |
| loose children over two slots | refused: "2 `List` fields its statements could fill — set each by name: `body: collect { … }`" |
| `body: collect { … }` over two slots | works |
| `actions { … }` as a block over two slots | refused, same words |
| `@default` on a slot | refused: `type.component_event`, "only an event can be a component's default" |
| `@children` on a composition | refused: "a composition projects nothing of its own" |
| `item { key: n }` with an `int` | refused: `key` is `string?` |

### Context

Owner decisions of 2026-11-17. A context (`ctx`) is for any Avra code, not only UI.

- **It is a language feature**, not a library type. Rejected: a library `context(default)` with
  `.set(v) { … }` and `.get()` over the task's slots as a first step.
- **Declared once, by name, with a type**: `ctx log: Logger = quiet_logger`. A context with no
  default is required: a reader no path has set it for is a compile error.
- **Set by a line that lasts to the end of its block**, as `let` and `defer` do, never by a new
  block: `ctx log = file_logger("app.log")`. Several are several lines. A context may be a
  record, so what travels together is one name (`theme` holds colour, space, density, mode).
- **A set nothing after it in the block reads is a warning** (`if debug { ctx log = verbose }`
  sets nothing once the `if` ends).
- **The value lives on the running task for the block** (candidate B: Java's `ScopedValue`, Swift's
  task-locals, Avra's own `within`). Rejected: a hidden parameter on everything that reads it
  (a child would see where it was written, not where it is placed; fn types would carry it); tree
  inheritance alone (UI only); the 2026-10-04 `ambient` seat (fills from the caller's scope alone,
  so every component in between declares it: drilling).
- **What runs later keeps the context it was made under** (a handler, a closure), laid over the
  context it runs under: a context set where it was made reads as it was set there, and one set
  nowhere it was made reads as where it runs (ruled 2026-11-19: a handler built in setup and run by
  a worker under the worker's set reads that set). A task the block owns inherits; a detached one
  starts from the defaults. A call arriving from C starts from the defaults. In UI the walk sets
  the value as it enters where the line stands, so a child sees where it is placed. A required
  context is judged at every entry, at compile time: the program's statements, each spec case and
  each host fn.
- **Deadlines stay `within`**, apart from values.

- **The word in code is `ctx`**, for all three: `ctx log: Logger = quiet_logger` declares,
  `ctx log = file_logger("app.log")` sets, `ctx.log` reads. A read wears the word so a reader
  tells three sources apart at sight: `self.x` is the component's own, `ctx.x` comes from the
  surroundings, a bare name is the file's or a local. Rejected: a bare read (it looks like a
  constant); a symbol (`@` is annotations and packages, `$` interpolation, a leading dot an enum
  variant; `^`, `~`, `#` say nothing); the long word `context` at every read.

Settled in the design review of 2026-11-18 (the lane's design: `avra-lane-ui-language-ctx.md`):

- **The value is a frame on the task**: one entry per declared `ctx`; a set copies the frame, a read
  is one load. A caught trap puts the guard's frame back by one store.
- **A change repaints by structure.** What a set's value reads are structural reads of the
  composition holding the set, so a write repaints that subtree whole; an exact patch never shows a
  stale context value. A child repainting alone runs under the frame its instance was drawn under.
- **A handler on a node runs under the frame the node was drawn under**, as a view does; a plain
  closure or a fn value keeps the frame where it was made.
- **The required check is sound through `dyn` calls**: a `dyn T` call counts as reading every `ctx`
  any impl of `T` reads (relayed by the compiler lead as the owner's ruling; measured: no refusal
  added on std-ui or the board). It runs at entries; `explain` shows an exported fn's reads.
- **A cached fn reads only a `ctx` that compares by value**; its key adds each read's value. One
  holding fns or cells is refused inside a cached fn. Built (2026-11-20) as one mechanism, exact on
  every path: a `ctx` read inside a running `@query` body is one of its answer's reads, as a Db read
  is, and the answer stands only while each context it read holds what it held (std-relation's
  kernel, `runtime/avra_runtime.c`). A query reused inside another is heard by it. A read outside a
  query tests one runtime word and does nothing else; the filing is the cold path, out of line. A
  context told apart by no word (a nullable set, a record, a list) keeps no answer; its refusal at
  compile time is not built.
- **No implicit default**: `ctx x: T? = null` is written out. `theme`'s default is the target's own
  default theme. A `ctx` is imported by name and shares the top-level namespace.

Owed before it is built, each as a real program: a cache keyed by a fn's inputs counts its
context reads; "this fn reads `db`" computed lazily, so a deep edit does not ripple through every
caller; a trap caught by a guard restores the context (on wasm a caught trap runs no cleanup
today); a lint against a context where a parameter would do.

The prior art read was Odin and Jai
`context`, Scala implicits and givens, Kotlin context parameters, Java `ScopedValue`, Go
`context.Context`, React Context, SwiftUI `@Environment`, Compose `CompositionLocal`, Flutter
`InheritedWidget`, Clojure `binding`, OCaml 5 effects.

### Theming

Owner decisions of 2026-11-17. Study: `.lead/archive/ui/studies/theming_prior_art.md`.

- **Two kinds of look, and the compiler says which.** *Native*: the platform draws the control,
  and a theme says only what every platform honours (roles, accent, light and dark, contrast, text
  roles that scale, spacing, density, icons, surface levels). *Drawn*: the author builds the control
  from primitives and owns every pixel, by an explicit choice. A style that asks a native control
  for what a target cannot honour (a radius on an iOS button, a bar background, a shadow) is
  reported per target; no target drops it in silence.
- **The app's look changes when the OS redesigns.** That is what native by default means.
- **An accent follows each platform's own rule.** The theme carries one brand accent, as a request.
  An app that sets none gets what the system or the user chose. One that sets it has it wherever
  the platform lets an app decide (iOS, Android), and where the platform keeps that for the user
  (macOS standard controls) the compiler says so, as it does for any style a target will not honour.
  A user's accessibility setting (more contrast, larger text) wins over both.
- **The web has a designed look of its own.** It sits on the system font, the OS's dark mode,
  contrast and reduced-motion settings, and real form controls underneath. Rejected: imitating the
  platform the browser runs on (it lags the real thing, as a self-drawn toolkit does); the browser's
  unstyled controls (dated, and different in each browser).
- **The web's default look starts from "Crisp"** (chosen by eye from three directions,
  2026-11-17, to be edited as we go): black and white, the accent is the ink, 4px corners, no
  shadows, 36px controls, the system font. Its values, light and dark, are the `crisp` table in
  the canvas https://claude.ai/artifact/VMepQxHkJ1vdSgfSmRGTaz (`project/Main.dc.html`). The bar:
  useful in many settings and good out of the box. The three directions were one markup under
  three sets of about thirty-five values, which is the property wanted of every theme.

- **A theme is a typed record.** A misspelled role is a compile error, where a text-keyed map
  answers an empty value.
- **A role's value may be "what the system uses"**, which is how the default theme is native.
- **Colour roles come as a background and what sits on it**, so every pair's contrast is proved at
  build time, in every mode (against WCAG 2.x).
- **Light and dark, contrast and text size are dials the OS turns**, not separate themes.
- **About fourteen colour roles**: canvas, surface at three levels, accent, neutral, danger,
  success, warning, text, muted, disabled, border, focus, link.
- **A state's look (hover, pressed, disabled) is derived from its role**, so one brand colour moves
  every state; on a native control the state is the OS's.
- **A theme reaches a subtree through `ctx theme`.**

Tokens run through every component, theme and target. Built on `program` (2026-11-17, theming
slice 1, `b22082cb6`..`f156e652c`): a theme is typed tables, one field per role, read through
`@derive(Table)`'s `at(k)`, so a missing role does not compile; colour roles are surfaces (canvas,
surface, raised, sunk, accent, neutral, danger, success, warning), each a fill and what sits on it,
and inks (text, muted, disabled, danger, success, warning, link, border, field, focus); Crisp is the
default; a colour is a `Dial { light, dark, more }`, so light and dark are one theme and more
contrast is a second dial, both following the OS on a served page and a live one unless `Mode` or
`Contrast` holds one; `text_size`, `line_height`, `tracking` and `elevation` reach the web.
Slice 2 (`d807476c5`, `e516d7760`): the default look of controls is theme data, a `Look` per
primitive (its surface, ink, border, radius, least height, padding; what it leaves unsaid is the
target's own), and one rule, `worn`, derives a state from it (hover and pressed shade the surface by
`shading`, disabled wears the disabled ink, focus keeps the platform's ring), so the web target keeps
only plumbing; contrast is proved at compile time by a validating annotation, `@legible(theme)`,
over 49 pairs in light and dark at standard and more contrast (words 4.5:1; a control's border, the
focus ring and the disabled ink 3:1; the divider is held decorative and owes nothing, a call the
owner has not ruled on), each refusal naming the pair, the two colours, the ratio and the bar. Crisp
passes whole; its tightest pair is the disabled ink on the sunk surface, 3.08:1.
Still not so: no `ctx theme`; the terminal drops the theme; the first frame of a mounted page is
3,721 octets (1,454 before theming) and the board's module 354,600 bytes (304,180 before typed
routes and theming); nothing automated shows that a theme missing a role is refused. Values chosen
in the slices, not by the owner: the warning, neutral and link colours, a type scale of
32/26/17/15/12, hover and pressed shading of 8 and 16.

Built (2026-11-17, `17b3819d1`, `2cf79133c`): an event names no target. A component declares
`on press`; each target keeps one table of what delivers each primitive's events
(`realize/html/delivery.av` over `DomEvent`, `terminal/delivery.av` over `Key`), a seat with no
row or two is refused at build, and a third target adds one `delivery.av` and touches no component.
Lost on the way: a mistyped event in a table is the compiler's own variant refusal, which lists
every variant where the old voice said "did you mean".

Nothing is left to build in std-ui for the first milestone without the compiler. Waiting on the
compiler: `words` and `reads` out of the primitives (the compiler makes an instance's `words`
closure only from fields written in the component itself; moved into a shared spread, the exact path
declines every turn), `state` in a component head, `@default` on a slot and a slot filled by a named
block, a key of any comparable type, `ctx`.

Owed elsewhere: CLAUDE.md's "subset" section says a keyed list (`list xs by it.id`) is refused and
that `@model` is undefined; both work (`std-ui/src/tests/keyed_list`, `packages/std-db/src/db.av`).

### How a native target draws

Leading, not proved (2026-11-17; study `.lead/archive/ui/studies/native_rendering_prior_art.md`).

- **iOS: UIKit is the spine, SwiftUI stands where Apple requires it** (widgets, Live Activities).
  One differ runs, ours; our solver places, and UIKit measures text and controls; the default path
  needs no Swift compiler; standard UIKit controls take an OS redesign on recompile. Rejected as the
  spine: handing the tree to SwiftUI, whose case was that applying patches re-implements diffing
  (we diff anyway), and whose nearest shipped product, LiveView Native, was archived in February
  2026. The earlier ruling "UIKit and Android Views through the C ABI" holds for iOS only.
- **Android: Compose behind a small Kotlin shim, our differ in charge.** Google put Views,
  RecyclerView and Fragments into maintenance on 2026-05-19, so driving Views directly is a dead end.
- **On every target:** the platform's own navigation and sheets, with a back-swipe or a dismissal
  arriving as an event; the platform's list recycling under stable keys; text input tested hardest,
  since it breaks first everywhere. From the iOS 27 SDK an app without the scene lifecycle does not
  launch.
- **Settled by a spike, its rule fixed first:** one screen of the board (10,000 rows, a search field,
  a push, a sheet, an alert) built on UIKit and on SwiftUI over the same Avra diff; UIKit wins if it
  matches on text input, identity, navigation and accessibility and is ahead on scroll, update time,
  size and startup; SwiftUI is reconsidered if it wins text input or identity outright. It waits on
  an iOS compiler target.

Recorded for later, after the above is right: the primitives a real app lacks (select, radio,
multi-line text, tabs, table, scroll, menu, tooltip, toast).

### Milestone 1: rulings of 2026-11-18 to 2026-11-24

The board on the web, written the decided way. Each line is a ruling of the UI lead unless it says
owner.

- **`shown: T? = null` is display only.** Where given, the control shows it instead of its place;
  it never writes. Revisited at the sync study, where an optimistic value and a confirmed one differ.
- **A member's relation to a place is a mark**: `@over(place)` on what is shown instead of it,
  `@sets(place[, constant])` on the event that writes it. `@seeds` is dropped: a place's first value
  is its declaration's (`state checked: bool = self.initial`).
- **The head form is the one form.** `tab "All" { chosen: filter, shows: .All }`: what the head
  declares goes in the head, the rest in the block. Naming a head seat in the block is refused, and
  the refusal carries the edit.
- **A `state` field in a type is a shared reference**, opt-in, field by field. Its cost on a row
  drawn ten thousand times is unmeasured.
- **No `derived` keyword.** A pure fn that reads state is cached by the compiler, keyed by what it
  read. A one-day study fixes the rule (what counts as pure, where the cache lives, the bound).
- **`ctx theme` is built**: std-ui declares it, the board sets it once at its page, and a theme's
  scope on the page carries no hash. `ctx_reads` is computed lazily. Owed: the receipt for that
  laziness, the lint for a `ctx` a parameter would do for, and the 82 more boxes a paint makes.
- **The scheme is three choices**, Auto, Light, Dark, over `state mode: Mode = .System`. A two-way
  toggle between "as the system is" and "dark" changes nothing on a dark device.
- **A field hears `on submit`**, with no payload: the field's place already holds the text. Enter
  or a phone's return key fires it, never mid-composition, never in a multi-line field. The key's
  word is `submit_label: SubmitLabel = .Done` (`Done`, `Go`, `Send`, `Search`), one concept on the
  web, iOS and Android. Setting it with no `on submit` is refused. `Next` waits on focus order.
- **A page says a failure in plain words.** "This page could not start." or "This page stopped
  working."; the error's own words follow under `avra dev` only, and a built page offers a reload.
  An `error` event with no error object and no file is a script the page does not own: never drawn,
  told to the console, and under dev carried to the server's output with the browser's name.
- **The browser is a gate slice** (`ui-browser`): the board's claims run in a real browser on every
  merge.

### Lessons from the owner's first look (2026-11-24)

Three defects reached the owner on a board whose 96 browser claims were green.

- **A claim asserts what the reader sees.** Every dark-mode claim asserted "no error drawn", on a
  light-scheme browser. The toggle changed nothing on a dark phone and every claim passed. A claim
  about a look reads the drawn colour, under each scheme the OS can ask for.
- **A test runs under every dial the OS turns**: scheme, contrast, text size, pointer or touch,
  keyboard. One default configuration is one sample.
- **The keyboard is an input.** Enter in the new-task field did nothing and no claim pressed it.
  Each control's claims drive it by pointer, by touch and by key.
- **A test's side effects are checked.** A script that "added a task" never read the task list, so
  an add that saved nothing passed.
- **The lead opens the page before the owner does**, in a browser, under both schemes, and says
  what was not covered (a browser we cannot run is named, not implied).
- **A refusal with no place is a defect.** A `state` of one enum in a seat of another passed
  `check` and failed the build with "a seat refuses a value here", no line. Filed.
- **A dev loop's broken edit must not outlive the edit.** A machine-wide store kept a null under a
  good file's key after an unterminated fn, and every later build of the untouched board failed.
  Fixed, with a witness (good, broken, fixed, fresh copy).

### The storyboard (planned 2026-11-24, owner)

Every component in every state, for the owner's eye and for the gate. It grows out of
`@std/ui_gallery` (entries, cases, semantic snapshots, `changed`), which stays the one definition.

- **The list of components is the compiler's**, never written by hand: a component with no story
  is refused.
- **A story stands beside its component** and is gathered across files.
- **States are derived from the component's own members** (each `bool`, each enum, disabled,
  empty, long text), and each is drawn under each dial (scheme, contrast, text size) on each target.
- **One set of stories, three uses**: the page the owner inspects, the gate's claims and
  snapshots, and the parity check between the web and a native target.

### Native targets: what the iOS spike settled (2026-11-25)

The spike (branch `ios-spike`; findings `docs/2026_11_25_IOS_SPIKE_FINDINGS.md`, design
`docs/2026_11_25_NEUTRAL_PATCH.md`) runs the board's own `view`, unedited, as UIKit controls in the simulator.
Rulings of the UI lead unless a line says owner. Targets and the manifest: `docs/2026_11_25_TARGETS.md` (owner).

- **The platform's loop owns the main thread; the program and its tasks are fibers on it.** A run-loop
  source drives a budgeted turn; idle returns to the host. One mechanism with wasm's hosted idle. Measured:
  a task hop 0.19 µs, a thread hop 3–6 µs. A text field's delegate is answered inside its call; a handler that
  waits there is refused.
- **No target is decided from another target's output.** A native host never reads HTML. The spike's first
  host did, guessing controls from tags, and every defect on its screen came from that.
- **One differ, generic over what it diffs.** The matcher (`realize/diff/`) reads a trait: an element's step,
  id and what it keeps. Each target lowers `Node` to its own elements and owns its patch type. The web's
  lowering and wire are unchanged, held byte-identical. Rejected: one element type whose kind is a sum (the
  web pays a box per element); a shared patch type (the web pays a box per patch).
- **A native element is one per primitive**: its prim and its FACTS, a fact being a typed code, the
  interaction it holds under, and a scalar (text, flag, number, fraction). One exhaustive projection each for
  `Attribute`, `Style`, `Layout` and `Theme` is the element's equality, its wire and the key of the host's
  table. A new attribute or style field breaks the build until the native target answers it.
- **On a native target our solver places and the platform measures.** The host tells the program "measure
  again" (width, text size, safe area) as one event; the values stay behind `Measure`.
- **The device's appearance, contrast and text size are the host's to resolve.** The theme's mode and
  contrast travel as facts, so a program's own choice of scheme reaches the host as data.
- **"Chosen" is data, never only a fill.** Whether `button` gains `selected` or a choice among N becomes a
  primitive (the platform's segmented control) is open.

Open, found by the spike: a base address for a native target's routes; start-of-app work reachable from every
entry (one `mount` removes the drift); a native stream for a feed (`Want.Listen` is refused natively, so no
live updates); work that must leave the UI thread (a spinning task left the display 7 frames where idle had
339, longest gap 408 ms).

### The performance bar (owner, 2026-11-25)

World class is a set of numbers a gate holds, measured against a hand-written UIKit app, on a device.

| Bar | Number |
|---|---|
| One changed value | reaches the screen with no tree rebuilt and no allocation |
| A frame's work | fits 8 ms (120 Hz) on a mid-range phone |
| 10,000 rows | no dropped frame in a fling; memory follows the rows on screen |
| Against hand-written UIKit | within 10% on cold start, memory and update time |
| Counters per change | allocations, patches, measure calls: recorded, and only shrink |

Known costs when the bar was set, each to be measured before it is fixed:

- A native paint rebuilds the tree, lowers it and diffs it; the web's exact path is not wired for native.
- Each element's facts are a fresh list every paint; a paint makes 82 boxes more since `ctx theme`, unexplained.
- The spike's frame is text the host parses; the web's wire is binary.
- The solver places the whole tree each paint, and nothing caches a text's measure.
- A task that never waits starves the display: the budget does not cut it.
- The web's module is 318 KB, uncensused. The web's first paint: 1416 ms → 706 ms on a 120 ms / 4 Mbit
  link; the server-drawn first paint is built, not landed.

Order (owner): placement by our solver first, so what is measured is the real shape; then the benchmark (the
board at 10,000 rows, a hand-written UIKit twin, the owner's phone) before any more features; then fixes in
the order the numbers give; the SwiftUI twin after.

- `HARVEST`: one small piece to extract.

### c1a6a85342 RESTART — board-server
refs: board-server, board4, origin/board-server; subsumed: board-server3, origin/weak-builtin-name, weak-builtin-name
what: Moves the board's tasks to a @crud server. Also: sqlite reopens after a fork, crud returns 201 + Location, `Db` doors take no `mut`, and the page names its module on documentElement. The compiler change: a program's own `type Task` binds over the built-in constructor's bare name, while `Task<T>` still reaches the spawn handle.
evidence: The board, crud and sqlite work all landed in later forms. program's std-crud routes.av has list/find/create/update/remove/trash/restore. sqlite/open.av reopens per process after the cores' fork. dev.js sets data-avra-module. browser.mjs has "a page loaded again shows it". The Task-name idea did NOT land: program still refuses it (compiler/tests/subset/refusals_test.av:343 pins "error[resolve.builtin_type]: `Task` is a built-in type"), and CLAUDE.md's subset lists it as a refusal.
keep: Idea: "a constructor row's bare name is a program's own to declare". Built-in names split into two classes:
  - Names the LANGUAGE owns outright: scalars and non-settlement shapes such as `Bytes`. These keep refusing `type Bytes = …` (resolve.builtin_type), because a name would resolve to the shape in annotations and to the declaration elsewhere.
  - CONSTRUCTOR rows such as `Task`. Their bare name binds WEAKLY: a file's declaration wins, and the applied form `Task<T>` still reaches the row.

  How the branch did it:
  - typing.av: `shape_type_name(name)` = `shape_named(name) != null`. The refusal fires only for these names.
  - resolve/walk.av: a language declaration binds last. A template's type name binds first to the declaration of the file it lands in, then to the language's own (63edf945e).

  Witnesses to rebuild:
  - features/tasks/tests/own_task_name: `type Task = {id,title}` with `spawn work()`, eval == native.
  - std-json/tests/own_task_name: a derive (`from_json_text`) builds the program's own Task.
  - Two refusal cases: `Task<int>` beside own `Task` checks clean; `type Bytes` still refuses.

  Why: domain models are commonly called Task (the board's model). This needs an owner decision, because program deliberately pins the refusal. Commits: da11263c8, 63edf945e.

### 3a2a839050 RESTART — origin/archive/ui/eqstruct
refs: origin/archive/ui/eqstruct
what: Structural `==`/`!=` design doc (avra-channels-briefs/structural_equality.md, CHANNELS avra-8sb5.34.53.29) plus a held prototype marked "DO NOT MERGE" (owner 2026-10-06: law for the future, do not build until the owner says so).
evidence: Absent from program. CLAUDE.md subset: "`==` … compare a scalar, text, a tagged enum, or a one-field record; two fields is refused … an enum carrying a payload is refused". The doc is not in program.
keep: Idea, for when the owner says go. Make `==` a deep structural value compare.
  Semantics:
  - Records: field by field, in declaration order.
  - Lists: equal length, then element-wise in order.
  - Maps: entry count plus key lookup, ORDER-INSENSITIVE. This was an owner call: insertion order is a projection, not identity.
  - Payload enums: tag, then the payloads.
  - Nullables: via the existing `same_through`. Named types are transparent (`seen_through`).
  - Recursive types: REFUSED by name with a new voice `type.recursive_compare` ("a deep walk would follow the cycle forever"). Cycles are constructible through `mut` lists, and the runtime keeps no visited set.
  - Still refused: Cell/Task/Fn/dyn/type params/Range/Ptr.

  One predicate:
  - `compares_by_value` widens `contains`, `index_of` and literal patterns together. There is no second equality.

  Cost and engines:
  - O(size to first difference), with short-circuits. The map compare snapshots keys (`avra_map_keys`).
  - Lowered entirely to IR (field/payload reads plus the existing Walk/turn_open/turn_elem/walk_done loop with a bool cell). There is NO new IR instruction and NO new runtime row, so eval == native holds by construction.

  Prototype result:
  - It printed identical output in both engines for a record with list+string, a nested record, a payload enum, a list of records, maps in two insertion orders, and a nullable list.
  - Suite NOT certified.

  Extract:
  - avra-channels-briefs/structural_equality.md (the full probe table of today's refusals and the design).
  - Prototype files: features/checks.av (law + recursion guard), features/values.av (`same_record/same_list/same_map/same_enum`), expr_spine/lower.av dispatch, lists/methods.av, and the expr_spine/tests/eq_structural witness.

### e2547c247a RESTART — refs/uisave/avra-generic-events-wip
refs: refs/uisave/avra-generic-events-wip
what: Uncommitted save (272 lines, 20 files) of "conditional impls": `impl Sh for List<T: Sh>` / `impl Sh for Box<T: Sh>`. The impl serves its target only where the argument meets the bound, and the body may call the argument's members.
evidence: Absent from program: no `bound_unmet`, `impl_bounds` or `itparam`. CLAUDE.md still says `impl T<K: Tr, V>` is "expected `{`". The touched files changed by ~2.7k lines since the save's base (6854559f3), so it will not rebase cleanly.
keep: Idea: conditional (bounded) trait impls, Rust's `impl<T: Sh> Sh for List<T>`.
  Grammar:
  - `impl X for Name<T: Bound, …>` via a new `itparam = name:NAME ( ":" ty:type )?` (ParamSyntax).
  - The bounds are stored per impl statement (`store.impl_bounds`, fingerprinted with `restamp_carrying(s, 200, …)`, rebuilt in rebuild.av).
  - `parts.decl_bounds` answers an ImplDecl's bounds.

  Typing:
  - Decls records `ImplRow { of: trait, block: impl }` per type, instead of a bare trait list.
  - `implements_type(ty, d, tr)` checks every bound the impl writes against the type's own args (`bound_met`), so `List<Order>` is Decode when Order is.
  - `TypeCx.owner_impl` is set while walking an impl's method body, so a `T`-typed receiver answers the impl's bound (`seat_bound`, `seat_bound_args`).
  - A method call on a receiver whose args miss the impl's bound is refused with new kind `type.bound_unmet`: "`List<int>` has `said` only where `int` implements `Sh`". Help: "implement `Sh` for `int` — `impl Sh for int { … }`". A remedy row and a witness are in dev/remedies.av and witnesses.av.

  Witnesses:
  - features/impls/tests/conditional_impls (Box<T: Sh>, List<T: Sh>, a generic fn `shown_as<X: Sh>`, and dispatch through `dyn Sh`; expected output in the save).
  - conditional_impls_test.av.

  State: uncommitted, gate status unknown. Rebuild it on the current pipeline (std-program if it now owns impls).

### 8322bb7d14 RESTART — origin/ui-wasm-eh
refs: origin/ui-wasm-eh, ui-wasm-eh; subsumed: origin/ui-guard-3, ui-guard-3
what: Trap unwinding through `defer` cleanups. The native and evaluator half LANDED: `Ins.Cleanup`, invoke plus landing pads in llvm_emit.av, and the cleanup_attacks and cleanup_unwinding_trap tests. The WASM half did not.
evidence: program backend/llvm.av:67 says "a wasm module has none: `unwinds` is false there". llvm_wrapper.c has no `CleanupPad`, funclet call or `__gxx_wasm_personality_v0`. Only avra_runtime.c is built with wasm EH, for setjmp. There is no tools/wasm-fixtures/unwind or unwind_attacks. docs/2026_10_05_UI_ARCHITECTURE.md lacks the "What holds on each target" section.
keep: Rebuild "a trap caught by a guard runs the frames' deferred calls on wasm" (commits 45ca3e3f1, 01438ffa9, 694b9a82f, 7e45d8c33):
- In a wasm module, calls under an open Cleanup are `invoke` as on native. The unwind block is a cleanup FUNCLET: `cleanuppad` (parent none), each call inside it carrying a `funclet` operand bundle, then `cleanupret` to the caller. These are C wrappers `avra_llvm_build_cleanup_funclet`, `_funclet_call` and `_cleanup_ret`, enabled with `-wasm-enable-eh -exception-model=wasm -wasm-use-legacy-eh=false` (the standard exnref encoding).
- The guard's longjmp is a wasm exception, so each frame's funclet runs on the way to the guard. The runtime defines `__gxx_wasm_personality_v0`. `+exception-handling` joins the wasm target features and wasm-opt flags.
- Page host: validate a probe module and refuse an engine without exnref EH by name (Chrome 137, Firefox 131, Safari 18.4).
- Measured: the funclets cost +56 B on a two-defer bench; the board grew +8,056 B, from runtime rows, not pads.
- Witnesses: wasm-fixtures/unwind and unwind_attacks (nested guard, trap at a normal exit, extern row deferred, 3000 frames), eval == native == wasm.
- Doc: add "What holds on each target" to UI_ARCHITECTURE (a caught trap ends the turn, not the process; the turn's writes stand; defers run innermost-first; a trap on another task, a stack overflow and process limits are never caught).

### 2e448a94f5 RESTART — origin/archive/ui/origin-wasm-flat
refs: origin/archive/ui/origin-wasm-flat
what: perf(wasm32): flat child rows in a settled static. A settled static's scalar child rows are laid out as bare cells (no Header, no AvraArray) when a whole-program pass proves every read of their address is a constant-index cell read that never escapes as a box. Such reads go through a new `avra_flat_get` row.
evidence: no `avra_flat_get`, `flatten_rows`, lower/flat.av or flat_rows fixture in program (only the UI_ARCHIVE_INDEX line).
keep: Idea for a rebuild in program's static layout (features/statics.av, `Ins.StaticAddr`):
- A whole-program pass decides per static. Candidates are statics where EVERY element a root read can produce is a non-empty scalar row (uniform, so one rewrite is right for every runtime index).
- A static ESCAPES if any read of a child address is stored, passed to a call, length-read or read managed. One escape keeps the row boxed.
- The same walk rewrites each proven-safe constant-index cell read into `avra_flat_get`; the static's flat boxes are emitted headerless.
- Proof: tools/wasm-fixtures/flat_rows plus features/tests/flat_rows plus flat_mark_test (eval == native == wasm).
- Not recorded: any size or speed measurement. Measure the wasm board's static bytes before adopting.
- Check against the header law: a flat row has no header, so nothing may retain or release it, which is what the escape analysis guarantees.

### dfbf33c1fa RESTART — refs/remotes/pr/223
refs: refs/remotes/pr/223
what: A BUILT frame-boundary scheduler with priority lanes and a "hot channel" (`packages/std-ui/src/sched/sched.av`, plus tests). It landed as #223 and was later deleted.
evidence: Program has no sched/ and no Priority/Hot. UI.md records "Plan 4: the scheduler — batching, priorities, the hot-channel lowering. One-door loop with `Frame` built; priorities/hot channel not" (ledger 783) and "hatch: the HOT CHANNEL … evidence: none" (1277). The gestures gap (1601) depends on "scheduler #223". Program coalesces turns into one frame (native/sources.av:76, next_paint), but has no lanes and no hot channel.
keep: Rebuild over program's frame loop.
- **Writes are data.** A write never paints. It is an `Update { site, key, what }`, recorded and drained once per frame. `What` is Structure, Text(s), Flag(b) or Hot(Move). Nothing in the queue is a closure, so it stays comparable, serializable and inspectable.
- **Coalescing.** Keep the LAST write per identity (site, key, kind) in first-seen order. A flag and the words at one site are two writes, because the kinds differ.
- **Priority lanes.** `Priority { Input, Transition, Background }`. A boundary drains only the most urgent lane that has work, and later lanes wait for their own boundary. Input is never held behind rendered work.
- **Hot channel.** A KIND on the same queue, never a side door. `Move { Translate(x,y), Scroll(x,y), Scale(f), Opacity(a) }` is applied by the emitter to the live node without the tree. `Frame.renders()` is false when only hot work exists, so 60 pointer moves become one transform per frame and nothing re-renders.
- **Laws its tests proved** (sched_test + sched program test):
  - 4 writes to one place make 1 write;
  - repeated text keeps the last;
  - an empty scheduler answers null;
  - lanes drain input < transition < background, each at its own boundary, and lower lanes stay owed;
  - 60 hot moves make 1 direct write and no render;
  - hot plus structural is one frame that renders once.
- **Why it matters now.** It is the prerequisite for drag, scroll and gestures (DERIV gap 3), and for Transition/Background work such as observations refills not blocking input. Wiring needed: the hot channel needs a wire op (set transform/scroll) and a DOM host arm. Lanes need the app's turn queue to carry a priority.

### d9ecafb0b4 RESTART — refs/remotes/pr/222
refs: refs/remotes/pr/222
what: "The ONE update graph, and the DOM and CANVAS emit from it". A second emitter, `realize/canvas/canvas.av`, turns the same neutral ops into draw calls.
evidence: Program has no canvas target: no `realize/canvas`, and grep finds canvas only in DOM event tables. UI.md's ledger lists `canvas` as an escape primitive and says "no canvas", but not this BUILT emitter. The neutral update graph itself is superseded by program's Node tree, Avra diff and Patch ops.
keep: Idea: a canvas realizer, the target with no elements, proving the tree is not HTML-shaped.
- **Draw calls.** `enum Draw { Fill(x,y,w,h,color), Text(x,y,words,color,face), Rule(x,y,w,color), Block(x,y,w,h,color) }`, in paint order. `draw_text` renders it as readable lines for tests and `explain`.
- **Per-primitive form.** A `table<FormRow>` maps each Prim to a Form: Words, Bracketed (button `[x]`), Checkbox (`[ ] x`), Rule (divider), Block (image/icon), or Flow (containers). It is data, never a dispatch.
- **Colour and face** come from the theme: `fill` is drawn first and `color`/`face` default to Tone.Text/Font.Body.
- **Layout.** Its own flow layout in theme steps with fixed metrics (line 18, char 8). Rebuild it over program's `place/solve.av` with a `Measure` from `place/metrics.av`, so layout stays "one algorithm on every surface". The canvas then only maps placed boxes to Draws.
- **Hit-testing** is needed for events. tui/hit.av is the model.
- **Proof.** tests/draw: a column with a sunk fill, padding S, a heading and a button gave `fill 0 0 56 60 #ececec / text 8 8 Tasks … / text 8 34 [Add] …`. A wasm host would replay Draws onto a 2D context.
- **Doc claim to keep.** A canvas has no tags and the DOM no geometry. "Across all platforms" is one tree with different backends, never a lowest common denominator.

### 9ac7648ed5 RESTART — refs/heads/batch-crash
refs: refs/heads/batch-crash, refs/remotes/pr/877
what: Merges ui-wasm-eh, ui-guard-3 and ui-turn-guard. A trap caught by a guard runs `defer` cleanups on wasm (exnref EH). The page host validates a probe module and refuses an engine without EH by name. A doc section, "What holds on each target".
evidence: Absent from program. There is no `EH_PROBE` or `hasExceptionHandling` in runtime/dom/bootstrap.js, and backend/llvm.av:67 still says a wasm module does not unwind. The idea is ALREADY recorded in program docs/2026_11_16_UI.md as `8322bb7d14 RESTART — origin/ui-wasm-eh`. Do not file it twice.
keep: The rebuild is described in that UI.md entry. This ref adds two pieces to it:
- **Engine refusal.** bootstrap.js `EH_PROBE` is the smallest module needing exnref. `compiled()` turns a CompileError into "this browser lacks WebAssembly exception handling (exnref) — the app needs a current Chrome, Firefox or Safari" only when `WebAssembly.validate(EH_PROBE)` is false. Its three bootstrap_test cases cover: the probe validates; a stubbed engine names the feature; a compile error with EH present stays the engine's own.
- **Doc text.** A caught trap ends the turn, not the process. The turn's writes stand. Frames run their defer/errdefer innermost-first. A trap on another task, a stack overflow and the process limits are never caught. The default guard is `contained`, and a test may install `Screen.guarded`.

Sources: `EXPANSION` = `docs/2026_09_23_COMPONENT_EXPANSION.md`, `UI` = `docs/2026_09_29_UI.md`, `TOUR` = `docs/2026_09_29_UI_TOUR.md`, `WEB` = `docs/2026_09_29_WEB_UI.md`, `LIBRARY` = `docs/2026_10_01_UI_LIBRARY.md`, `COMPILED` = `docs/2026_10_02_COMPILED_UI.md`, `DERIV` = `docs/2026_10_04_UI_DERIVATIONS.md`, `ARCH` = `docs/2026_10_05_UI_ARCHITECTURE.md`, `ASSETS` = `docs/2026_10_05_UI_ASSETS.md`. ### components (123)

#### components — PLANNED (15)

- `empty { link("New board", to: form(Board)) }` — an empty-state slot on a collection. *[TOUR L140–143]* — evidence: none found
- Toolbar: `avatars(b.viewers)` (presence from `@model(presence)`), `picker(show)`, `menu { link("Whiteboard", to: whiteboard(b)); link("Invite", to: form(invite, b: b)); link("Ask", to: ask(b)); button("Delete board") { b.delete() } /* trash 30 d → undo, derived */ }`. *[TOUR L146–156]* — evidence: none found
- `canvas(b.shapes) { on frame(g) { …g.draw(s)…; for p in others(cursor) { g.cursor(p) } }; on hover(p) { cursor = p }; on drag(s: Shape, p: Point) { s.at = p } /* a draft while dragging, one write on release */ }` — shapes are its semantic peer. *[TOUR L178–186]* — evidence: no canvas; no drag gestures (DERIV gap 3)
- `screen settings() { section("Look") { picker(appearance) /* label from the name */; toggle(dense) /* this device only */ }; link("Boards Pro", to: upgrade()) }` — labels derived from binding names. *[TOUR L188–196]* — evidence: none found
- `view for Card { row { gap: if dense { .xs } else { .s }; toggle(done, title) /* a model field is a place: the write is Card.update */; spacer; badge(due?.relative()) { tone: if due?.past() ?? false { .danger } else { .muted } }; if hovered { fill: .surface_hover } } }`. *[TOUR L239–249]* — evidence: hovered is StateStyle data instead (std-ui/style/style.av); model-field-as-place absent
- Variants are one enum per family (`Kind`, `ControlSize`), never multiplying booleans; a variant a target cannot realize is refused for that target. *[LIBRARY L287–289]* — evidence: no Kind/ControlSize
- Slots are named blocks (dialog `actions`, app bar `leading`/`trailing`, field `prefix`/`suffix`); a slot is a typed fn, not a stringly key. *[LIBRARY L397–399]* — evidence: none found
- Preferences — values flow up: `title`, `toolbar`, `breadcrumb` deep in a page collected by the nearest ancestor asking. *[WEB L474–476]* — evidence: none found
- Still to design (S3+): a child no slot takes; `expand() -> Result<Decls, Diagnostic>` with `refuse_as_at` for a library's own meaning laws. *[EXPANSION L182–183]* — evidence: no `refuse_as_at` found
- A reserved word cannot bind, so such an instance names itself via `name:` (`command language { name: "grammar" … }`). DEFERRED with trigger: `run() { … }` code slots and typed `self.target` per command need the EXPAND stage (first user of S6 declaring blocks). *[EXPANSION L346–350]* — evidence: std-cli uses `run: dyn Runnable`
- §9: declaring blocks — a component may say its block is a record declaration (`model User { email: string }`), expanded as `@derive` over it. Later superseded for models by `@model type` (S6), but the general declaring-block mechanism remains unbuilt. *[EXPANSION L416–416]* — evidence: §8 S6; L580
- Infra sketch: `deploy production { region: "eu-west"  service api { runs: app, replicas: 3, memory: 512 MB }  database main { engine: postgres, size: 20 GB }  secret stripe_key }`. *[EXPANSION L476–481]* — evidence: no `component deploy`
- Gallery ask 1: declaring blocks — `model User { email: string }` holds TYPES after the colon vs `server app { port: 8080 }` VALUES; a declaring component receives a record DECLARATION (fields, marks like `@unique`) and expands like `@derive`. *[EXPANSION L500–505]* — evidence: L580 S6 pending
- S6 remaining: the expand stage and declaring blocks. *[EXPANSION L580–580]* — evidence: not found
- `@std/ui`'s own art (dialog close, checkbox tick — ~ten shapes, a few KB) sits in its own `art/` folder, tree-shaken like anything else; the open question is the app-facing icon vocabulary and default face. *[ASSETS L120–122]* — evidence: no std-ui art/

#### components — PARTIAL (30)

- Vocabulary table: content `text·heading·image·icon·video·badge·progress`; input `button·link·toggle·field·picker·slider·stepper·date·search`; collections `list·grid·table·section` (keyed, lazy by default); structure `row·column·stack·scroll·spacer·divider`; navigation `app sections·screen·toolbar·menu·tabs·split`; layers `sheet·alert·popover·toast·tooltip`; escape `canvas` (+ semantic peer) · `foreign` (typed platform view). *[UI L381–389]* — evidence: built Prims: text, heading, button, link, image, field, checkbox, row, column, dialog, divider, icon, surface, list, item, card, progress, alert, spinner, form, fieldset, switch, slider, key (+ error_text) — std-ui/tree/node.av; others absent; lists not lazy

- `screen inbox() { title "Due soon"; icon .tray; table(due_soon(), search: [.title, .board.name]) { columns: [.title, .board, .due] } }` — search field, `?q=`, debounce derived. *[TOUR L166–172]* — evidence: `model_table<T>` column per field (std-ui/forms/model_table.av); search/columns absent
- `view for Board { column { pad: .m; radius: .m; fill: .surface_raised; heading name; progress done_ratio(); if let s = site { map(s) { height: 120 }; text forecast(s).summary /* third-party, observed, cached */ } } }` — how a Board shows wherever it shows. *[TOUR L223–237]* — evidence: `Shows` trait (std-ui/tree/shows.av); tokens surface_raised/m exist (style/tokens.av); flat style keys and map absent
- `view card_detail(c) { scroll { column { gap: .m; heading c.title; image(c.photo, alt: c.title) /* absent → nothing */; field(c.note.text, "Private note") /* my account's row, made on first write */ …` *[TOUR L251–262]* — evidence: image requires `alt` or `decorative` (std-ui/components/image.av `image_words`); scroll/nullable image/place-bound field absent
- Bar: every component a real product needs (taxonomy §7), each with variants, sizes, states and an a11y contract. *[LIBRARY L18–22]* — evidence: 25 primitives (std-ui/tree/node.av Prim); no variants/sizes
- Shared groups `Look = { style }`, `Placed = { layout }`, `Named = { a11y: Name }`, `Keyed = { key: string? }`, and `Box = { ..Look, ..Placed, ..Named, ..Keyed }` which components spread. *[LIBRARY L260–269]* — evidence: `Box = { style, layout, a11y, ..Stand }` (Stand = key, site, via) in std-ui/style/box.av — one record, no sub-groups
- `component button(label: string) { ..Box; kind: Kind = .Filled; size: ControlSize = .Medium; icon: Icon?; trailing: Icon?; on_press: Action?; disabled: bool = false; loading: bool = false }`. *[LIBRARY L271–281]* — evidence: button has label head, `..Box`, disabled, loading, submits, `on press` (std-ui/components/button.av); no kind/size/icon/trailing
- States are universal and separate from variants: `ControlState` (`Enabled`, `Hover`, `Focus`, `Pressed`, `Disabled`, `Loading`, `Selected`, `Invalid`); a target maps states to visuals; `disabled`/`loading` feed it. *[LIBRARY L290–293]* — evidence: `Interaction` (Hover, Focus, Pressed, Disabled) in style/style.av; disabled/loading attrs
- Taxonomy (core | later): Text & content `text, heading, paragraph, label, code, icon, image, avatar, badge, chip, divider` | rich text, markdown, TOC; Actions `button, icon_button, link, fab, segmented, menu_button` | split button, speed dial, command bar; Selection & input `checkbox, radio, switch, slider, range, stepper, field, textarea, search, picker, combobox, otp, date_picker, time_picker` | color picker, file upload, masked input, mention, rich editor; Collections `list, item, section, table, card, accordion, tabs, tree` | data grid, virtualized list, carousel, timeline, kanban; Navigation `app_bar, toolbar, bottom_nav, nav_rail, breadcrumb, pagination, stepper (wizard), menu, sidebar` | command palette, split view, tabs-as-route; Structure `column, row, stack, grid, scroll, spacer, surface, section, aspect, safe_area` | masonry, portal, z_stack; Overlays `dialog, sheet, popover, menu, tooltip, toast, banner` | hover card, context menu, command menu, coach mark; Feedback `progress (linear/circular), spinner, skeleton, empty, error_state, alert, status` | notification center, inline validation summary; Forms `form, fieldset, field_row, error_text, help_text` | wizard, dynamic form from schema; Media `canvas, video, image_zoom` | maps, charts, editors; Escape `foreign, canvas (+peer), raw` | webview, custom draw. *[LIBRARY L374–386]* — evidence: built: text, heading, icon, image, divider, button, link, checkbox, switch, slider, field, list, item, card, column, row, surface, dialog, progress, spinner, alert, form, fieldset, error_text, key (std-ui/components); "dynamic form from schema" ≈ model_form/model_table (std-ui/forms)
- Templates (a user component rendering a list from data) use derive/`@std.meta` machinery — the L4 sugar row. *[LIBRARY L400–401]* — evidence: generic `model_form<T>`/`model_table<T>` (std-ui/forms); compositions derived via `@composes` (ARCH)
- Overlays carry the layer's obligations: scrim, focus trap, dismiss on escape/back/outside, focus restoration, anchor positioning, a `modal` flag a target honors. *[LIBRARY L424–426]* — evidence: dialog `showModal` (focus inside), `on dismiss` on key/outside press (std-ui/components/dialog.av, ARCH seam 1/4); anchor positioning absent
- Components need no RUNTIME object; provenance survives inlining via an `OriginChain` side table on semantic nodes, dependency holes and patch ops (inlining composes chains); dynamic instances add semantic identity beside origin, never instead. *[WEB L343–346]* — evidence: ARCH "Compositions and instance state" gives components instance state (tests/instances*); OriginChain absent
- Crossing components table: parent→child = arguments; child→parent = a `mut` seat (path writes), a `State<T>` seat (whole writes), an action, or a code slot (`on pick(d) { }`); subtree shares = `provide x: T = …` above, `env T` below, missing provider = compile error, env carries ACTIONS (`dismiss`, `open_url`, `refresh`); far apart = top-level `state … at <profile>`; shared truth = both read it; child→ancestor = preferences. *[WEB L360–369]* — evidence: args + `on` seats BUILT (docs/2026_10_18_EVENTS.md "an `on` seat IS a fn field"); `provide`/`env` not in std-ui (DERIV §I "Capabilities as ambience", docs/2026_10_04_SHARING_MODEL `ambient` planned); top-level state BUILT without profile
- `?` in a view → nearest `failed { }`; pending/empty/failed handled or take the theme default (listed); `lazy for`: virtualized long lists. *[WEB L809–811]* — evidence: four-state async arms (std-ui/src/app/async.av; DERIV §B) replace `failed { }`; `lazy for` not found
- Declaration syntax: `export component command { config { description: string = "" }  children { flags: List<flag>, args: List<arg> }  run: Stmts  fn expand() -> Decls { quote { … } } }`. Fields with defaults, typed child slots, a code slot and an optional expand. `config {}` and `children {}` blocks were later retired (S3d) — fields are bare lines and children route by element type. *[EXPANSION L40–48]* — evidence: features/components/mod.av component_decl grammar (members:cfield | cevent | `..` spreads | body:stmt)
- `children { }` typed slots: a nested block of that component lands in its slot in order (V2 auto-push); a block no slot takes refuses at the user's line naming the slots. Built as routing by element type (S4); the `children {}` block spelling is gone. *[EXPANSION L72–74]* — evidence: std-cli/src/cli.av (List<flag> fields routed)
- A CODE SLOT (`run: Stmts`, `body: Code`) holds user code; an instance fills it with `run() { … }`; a value-head component's whole block is its one code slot. Value-head block-as-body built (templates); named `run() { }` slots in data components deferred. *[EXPANSION L75–77]* — evidence: std-http/src/route.av
- No injected names: a code slot reads the instance through `self`; the expander makes the record type (`flag release` → `release: bool`) and the slot becomes its method; verify in S3 that user `self` binds to the generated method's receiver. Data components read via `self` in methods; generated per-command typed `self.target` was deferred. *[EXPANSION L159–162]* — evidence: L346-350 deferral
- A component with more than one children slot places them by name (`slot_name { … }`, each its own collect); the instance desugars to its record literal so every law is the record's and list's. Named-slot block spelling was replaced by `left: collect { … }` settings (S3b). *[EXPANSION L245–248]* — evidence: L283-285
- §9: named slot placement (`header { … }` when slots share a type) — replaced by `left: collect {…}`; `check()` built; `from` REVISED to `production with { … }`. *[EXPANSION L420–422]* — evidence: S3b/S3c notes

Superset body + `on` events built; `each`/`run` slots not. *[EXPANSION L506–509]* — evidence: components grammar `body:stmt`

#### components — BUILT (68)

- Shared settings by field spread: `export type Common = { style: Style = Style {}, attrs: Attrs = Attrs {} }` spread into `export component text(content: string) { ..Common; selectable: bool = true }`. *[UI L862–871]* — evidence: `..Box` (style, layout, a11y, ..Stand) in std-ui/style/box.av and every component
- P2 Semantics, never pixels: a component names an interaction contract; a target draws it; `button` is not a rectangle. *[LIBRARY L51–52]* — evidence: Prim + per-target lowering
- §6 Every component is a `component` type whose fields come from shared groups (spread) plus its own content — the single most important structural rule. *[LIBRARY L253–258]* — evidence: std-ui/components/*.av `..Box`
- Content comes first in the head and is required where the component cannot exist without it (`button(label)`, `image(src)`). *[LIBRARY L283–286]* — evidence: `button(label)`, `image(picture)`
- Children are `items: List<dyn View>` on containers only; layout is the container's field, never a wrapper node. *[LIBRARY L297–298]* — evidence: std-ui/components/column.av `@children items: List<dyn View>`
- The doc is in the component: semantics, a11y contract, states, per-target behaviour; the doc IS the catalogue entry. *[LIBRARY L303–305]* — evidence: component `//!` docs (e.g. components/key.av, dialog.av)
- §8 Children are a list of views; a container's layout field positions them. *[LIBRARY L393–396]* — evidence: components/column.av
- Custom components are compositions: a new `component` implementing `View`, drawing only through existing primitives; a genuinely NEW primitive is a library contribution or the `foreign`/`canvas` escape — the price of a target-neutral tree and closed versioned primitive set, keeping every target able to draw every tree. *[LIBRARY L402–408]* — evidence: ARCH Compositions; refusals `@std/ui:prim`, `@std/ui:primitive-body`; foreign/canvas absent
- A new primitive is a library contribution or `foreign`/`canvas`. *[LIBRARY L495–495]* — evidence: closed Prim; refusals in ARCH
- Receipt: views writable today (probed 2026-09-29, build/avra at 31b8e21, scratch package, eval == native): `column c { text "Tasks"; for x in xs { text x { tone: "muted" } }; button "Add" }` → `<div><p>Tasks</p><p>a</p><p>b</p><button>Add</button></div>`; already landed: data components, value heads (`text x`), bodiless instances (`button "Add"`), `for`/`if` among children, mixed children via `List<dyn View>`. *[WEB L2063–2083]* — evidence: std-ui/src/components/column.av, text.av, button.av; `component` declarations (std-avrac/src/features/components); `dyn View` (std-ui/src/tree/view.av)
- What a user writes: `cli avra { description: "…"  command build { flag release { short: "-r" } arg target { required: true } run() { … } } }` imported via `use @std.cli.{cli, command, flag, arg}`. The nested-block CLI shape is built; the `run() { … }` code slot with typed `self.target` was deferred (see L346-350). *[EXPANSION L8–21]* — evidence: packages/std-cli/src/cli.av (component flag/option/arg/command); packages/cli/src/commands/check.av `export command check`
- THE ONE IDEA: a component is a record type with defaults that a block instantiates; with an `expand()` method answering code the block becomes that code, otherwise the block IS the record. Libraries never write grammar; every component block has ONE shape parsed by the compiler. *[EXPANSION L28–33]* — evidence: packages/std-avrac/src/core/provided.av `enum Instancing { Template, Record, Refused, Spoken }`
- The answer type is the effect: `expand() -> Decls` generates declarations, `-> Code` an expression; no `@expand` annotation. A component with no `expand` is plain data (a record literal at run time); most of the vision's sugar needs nothing more. *[EXPANSION L35–38]* — evidence: packages/std-http/src/route.av `fn expand() -> Code`; features/components/mod.av "type.component_expand"
- A FIELD'S TYPE IS ITS MEANING: `Code`/`Stmts` hold user code spliced where the template writes it; any other type is a value. A typed `let` in the template states the law the user meets at the user's head ("`limit` declares `Duration`, this is `int`"). *[EXPANSION L65–69]* — evidence: features/components/template.av
- Fields are bare lines (`name: T = default`); `config { }` still parses and retires with S3's `key:` ladder (retired in S3d). *[EXPANSION L70–71]* — evidence: components/mod.av grammar has no `config`
- Head rule: no params (`component cli`) → instance NAME (`cli avra { }`); params (`component within(limit: Duration)`) → a VALUE (`within ms(5) { }`). *[EXPANSION L78–79]* — evidence: components/mod.av `primary = word:COMPONENT … head:NAME … | heads:expression`
- Methods in the declaration are the record's `impl` — ordinary methods (S3c later fixed that they were never registered). *[EXPANSION L80–80]* — evidence: std-ui/src/components/image.av `fn check()` inside component
- TYPE PARAMETERS: `component Queue<T>(head: T) { … }`; each parameter is its record's, pinned by the head's value or a field's; an instance may write type args after the word (`queue<Charge> charges { … }`, `Box<float> { held: 1.25 }`); one component at two pins is two records; unpinned parameter refuses by name; wrong targ count / targs on a non-generic record refuse (`type.struct_targs`); a bound `<T: Trait>` refused (bounds only on free fns); a parameter names no value; a TEMPLATE's type parameter cannot reach copied code, so a seated type parameter wants a record component. Note: bounds on components now land (`model_table<T: Decode>`). *[EXPANSION L81–97]* — evidence: features/structs/check.av "type.struct_targs"; components/tests/components_generic_test.av; std-queue `component queue<T>`; std-ui/src/forms/model_table.av
- The instance grammar, one shape: `<word> <name>? { ( key: value | <child> … { … } | slot() { … } | BREAK )* }` (named head) and `<word> <expression> { <statements> }` (value head). *[EXPANSION L99–104]* — evidence: components/mod.av primary rule
- `<word>` is an imported component name, a block word like a sublanguage's (use pre-scan + lexer word set); `component cli avra { }` keeps working with the keyword; lexer tags it `COMPONENT`, `NAME` still accepts it; F2081 refuses a binder that wears it. *[EXPANSION L106–110]* — evidence: components/mod.av `word:COMPONENT`
- Every exported component is a block word; one whose instance cannot stand (no block field, computing expand) refuses AT THE INSTANCE in library words; an instance inside the component's own module is no instance (pre-scan reads other modules only) — pinned, one refusal. *[EXPANSION L111–115]* — evidence: CLAUDE.md S3d note; components tests
- Config is `key: value` (struct-literal / named-argument spelling); `key = value` goes (40 sites, the syntax ladder). *[EXPANSION L116–117]* — evidence: S3d L300-307; features/mod.av `LanguageFeature lists { k: v }`
- Inner words (`command`, `flag`, `description`) are CONTEXTUAL; only the imported word is reserved, and only where imported. *[EXPANSION L118–120]* — evidence: std-cli usage
- Two stages table: no `expand` → data, never runs, anywhere, a record literal (children are list fields); `expand` = ONE `quote` reading only `self` → at PARSE by substitution, anywhere; `expand` computes → EXPAND stage in the evaluator, top level only, `@derive`'s path with `Decls` admitted. Substitution and data built; computing expand stage partial (declaring blocks / S6 not built). *[EXPANSION L131–137]* — evidence: core/provided.av Instancing.Template/Record
- Nothing evaluates inside a parse (no cross-module analysis dependence, no import cycle through an expander); a computing component in expression position refuses: "`cli` generates declarations — write it at the top level"; a substituting one works anywhere. *[EXPANSION L139–143]* — evidence: features/components/check.av
- The instance crosses to `expand()` as the record: config values are VALUES (must settle — literals, consts), children are records, code slots are opaque `Code`/`Stmts` handles (splice only). *[EXPANSION L144–146]* — evidence: features/components/template.av
- Hygiene already paid: template names resolve where the quote was written; binders are origin-keyed (`resolve.av`'s `keyed`); `within`'s `outer` never meets a user's; slot user code keeps the user's origin. *[EXPANSION L148–153]* — evidence: CLAUDE.md "A COPIED TEMPLATE'S SPANS…"; features/quote
- Written types are hygienic too: a template's `let limit: Duration` resolves in the library; a type through a HOLE (`TypeRef.spliced`, derive `${t}`) resolves where it lands; a copied template keeps its own file's spans (errors point home) and range ownership reads it as the instance's. *[EXPANSION L154–158]* — evidence: CLAUDE.md `store.spanned_elsewhere`

- S3 data components: one body rule — `key: value` settings then any statement; settings become fields, everything else becomes `collect { … }`, the one children slot's value. `collect` is CORE (lists): a list of every value its statements would discard, in run order; `for/while/if/match/let` ordinary; the hook is the expression statement, typed by the list literal law, lowered by comprehension growth; a lambda's statements are its own. *[EXPANSION L231–241]* — evidence: packages/std-avrac/src/features/collects/
- Heads: head fields → VALUE (`text "hi"`); none → NAME (`cli avra { }`) which BINDS at statement position and fills a declared `name` field; as a member of another instance it is a child and binds nothing; a block is optional (`secret stripe_key`); `c from base { key: v }` = `base with { … }` (settings only, children the base's). *[EXPANSION L249–254]* — evidence: components/mod.av grammar (bodiless `heads:expression` alt)
- S3a AS BUILT: no-`expand` component is `Instancing.Record`; instance is `Expr.Component` whose view is the record literal (printer keeps written form, `avra expand` shows the literal); head by position, settings by name, other statements collected into the one unnamed `List` field; a headless record named at a statement binds (`grid report { … }` is a marked `let`) and fills `name`; field laws are the record literal's. Two general fixes: a list literal RE-HEARS its seat (retyped under the seat's type; `dyn` slot boxes each element); `dyn` acceptance asks the trait DECLARATION (no need to import the provider's trait). *[EXPANSION L266–279]* — evidence: core/provided.av Instancing.Record; components/mod.av Expr.Component
- S3b AS BUILT: a kept LIST spreads inside `collect` (comprehension child = its rows) unless the collect holds lists; a NAMED instance (`secret api`) is kept as a child at any depth and binds for siblings; a record with two `List` fields takes each by name (`left: collect { … }`), no slot sugar since `name { … }` collides with a struct literal. *[EXPANSION L280–287]* — evidence: features/collects/
- S3c AS BUILT: `fn check() -> List<Diagnostic>` (F2114 holds the shape) runs at compile time over each instance reading only compiler-known values (literals, decls, consts, instance-bound names); a run-time-valued instance is not checked; a hidden `instance.check()` minted by the builder, SETTLED as a const at lowering, decoded with the annotation crossing's `Diagnostic` reader, spoken at the instance (F2115); a trapping check says it did not run. - Seam 3, A PRIMITIVE SAYS WHAT IT PROJECTS TO: closed set the library owns; each member says what it is — `@attr(.Label)`, `@children`, `@style(color)`, an event, or the spread `..Box`; `@derive(View)` refuses a member that says nothing; word AND type checked against the library's own declaration (`Prim`, `Attribute`, `EventKind`'s words, `Style`) via `type_exported` — no second table; saying twice refused; a user component is a COMPOSITION inventing no projection; no meaning read from a field's spelling. (`EventKind` itself later deleted per EVENTS doc.) *[ARCH L92–102]* — evidence: components/button.av `@derive(View) @prim(.Button)`; tree/view.av
- Compositions and instance state: a user component is a COMPOSITION — ITS BODY IS ITS VIEW; its `state` members are places each INSTANCE owns; author writes no `@derive`, no spread, no `fn view()`, no return type, and may use it in its declaring file. *[ARCH L384–390]* — evidence: tree/view.av:25 "The author writes no `@derive` and no spread"
- Example: `component counter(label: string) { state n: int = 0  text "${self.label}: ${self.n}"  button "+" { on press { self.n = self.n + 1 } } }`; `component task_row(todo: Todo) { state open: bool = false  item { button self.todo.title { on press { self.open = !self.open } }  if self.open { text self.todo.notes } } }`; `fn page() -> column { column { counter "left"  counter "right" { n: 10 }  for t in visible() { task_row t { key: t.id } } } }`. *[ARCH L392–416]* — evidence: std-ui/src/tests/instances, tests/board/board.av
- A body draws IN PLACE: a composition has no node of its own; what its body makes (none, one, several) stands under the composition's parent (`View.describe` answers a list); a page holds no element for it. *[ARCH L418–423]* — evidence: tree/view.av `describe`
- A tree has one root: a root that drew none or several nodes is held in a column — the one wrapper, only there. *[ARCH L432–433]* — evidence: tree/view.av `rooted`
- A primitive is the other kind: `@prim`, marked members, no body, drawn by its target; a declaration with both is refused by name. *[ARCH L434–435]* — evidence: tree/view.av:839 "stateful-primitive", primitive-body kinds
- Law 10: `tree_of(view)` is a tree standing alone (no paint before/after) for a document, gallery, test of one node. *[ARCH L494–495]* — evidence: tree/view.av:139 `export fn tree_of`
- Refused `type.component_body`: "a component is made of what its body holds, and nothing here says what `x`'s is" (no trait composes); "one trait says what a component's body is, and here `A` and `B` both do"; the composing derive's own refusals in its words under its package's kind; a derive that cannot run is `annotation.unsettled` at the component. *[ARCH L562–569]* — evidence: components/mod.av "type.component_body"
- Refused `type.component_head`: "the brace after an instance's head opens the instance's block, so `P { … }` is no record literal here". *[ARCH L570–571]* — evidence: components/mod.av

- Library refusals: `@std/ui:prim` "a view is a primitive or is made of views, and `x` is neither"; `@std/ui:primitive-body` "a primitive is drawn by its target, and `x` holds a body of its own"; `@std/ui:stateful-primitive` "a primitive draws what it is told, and `n` is a place it would keep"; `@std/ui:boxed-composition`, `@std/ui:marked-composition` — a composition spreads no `Box` and marks no member. *[ARCH L577–584]* — evidence: std-ui/src/tree/view.av:526,839,848

#### components — OVERRIDDEN (8)

- The component is the node: no `Node`, no `Role`, no `XProps`, no `leaf`; a component's fields are its props; a tree is `dyn View`. *[UI L860–861]* — evidence: ARCH seam 10 "One `View`, one `Node`": components project to a neutral `Node` via `@derive(View)` (std-ui/tree/node.av, tree/view.av); no Role/XProps/leaf (BUILT part)
- P1 The component is the node: fields are props, schema, docs, a11y contract; no parallel `Node`, no `Role` enum, no `XProps`. *[LIBRARY L48–50]* — evidence: ARCH seam 10 keeps one neutral `Node` (std-ui/tree/node.av) that components project to via `@derive(View)`; no Role/XProps (that part BUILT)
- Example: `state cart: Cart = Cart {} at .tab`; `fn cart_total() -> Money { cart.lines.sum(it.price * it.qty) }`; `view header() { row { logo() spacer badge "${…}" } }`; `view stepper(n: State<int>) { row { button "−" { on click { n = n - 1 } } text "${n}" button "+" {…} } }`. *[WEB L378–391]* — evidence: built spelling is `fn view() -> column { … }` with `on press` (std-ui/src/ui.av header); `view` keyword/`State<int>`/`at .tab`/`on click` not used
