- Value-head template example: `export component within(limit: Code) { body: Code  fn expand() -> Code { quote { { let limit: Duration = ${self.limit}; let outer = avra_fiber_within(limit.ms); defer avra_fiber_within_end(outer); ${self.body} } } } }`. The value-head template mechanism exists (std-http routes use it) but `within` itself is core again. *[EXPANSION L50–63]* — evidence: features/tasks/check.av `within_expr_type`; std-http/src/route.av
- §9 slot spelling: no parens — `run { }`, `on click { }`, `each email { }`; one superset block body classified by the declaration. `on` became declared events; `run {}` code slots unbuilt. *[EXPANSION L417–417]* — evidence: std-ui button.av `on press`
- §9 `within`: a component in `@std/time`, spelled `within ms(500) { … }`. Spelling kept but it is a core keyword again. *[EXPANSION L429–429]* — evidence: features/tasks/mod.av

### state (122)

#### state — PLANNED (41)

- §3.1 URL state IS the screen's parameters: instead of route table + query parsing + state store + per-platform deep-link config, `screen board(b: Board, state show: Show = .all)`; required params form the path, `state` params the query. *[UI L212–218]* — evidence: DERIV §A (planned, not built); std-ui/tree/url.av is a safe URL type only
- A model parameter encodes as its identity; the codec is derived, or the type is refused at the screen ("a profile's type fits it"). *[UI L219–220]* — evidence: none found
- Writing `show = .open` inside the screen REPLACES the navigation entry — that is `.url` state, with no `at .url`. *[UI L221–222]* — evidence: none found
- `link board(b)` and `go board(b)` are typed calls: a broken link is a type error; renaming a screen renames every link. *[UI L223–224]* — evidence: link takes a `Url` (std-ui/components/link.av, tree/url.av)
- The `.url` profile retires; `at .url` on a body `state` is refused: "a screen's URL is its `state` parameters — move `show` into the signature". *[UI L229–231]* — evidence: no `at` profiles
- `link board(b)` / `go board(b)` push; back gesture, browser history, Android predictive back and iOS swipe-back are ONE stack. *[UI L537–538]* — evidence: none found
- Decision 7: state profiles: drop `.server`, `.tab`→`.window`, add `.account` (recommend yes). *[UI L668–669]* — evidence: no profiles exist
- Rule: a control's first prop is a PLACE (state, a `state` param, a model field): `toggle(dense)` · `picker(show)` · `field(c.note.text, "Note")`. *[UI L700–700]* — evidence: controls take `value:`/`checked:` settings + handlers (ARCH "What the page keeps"); first head is a label (std-ui/components/field.av)
- Rule: a destination is a value — a screen call, a row (its `home()`), a `form(…)`: `link(to: i.board)` · `remind(…, open: c)`. *[UI L704–704]* — evidence: link takes a `Url`
- `impl Card { fn home() -> Screen { board(self.board, open: self) } }` — a row's home screen is a destination. *[TOUR L81–83]* — evidence: none found
- `screen board(b: Board, state show: Show = .all, state open: Card? = null)` — route + query state incl. a nullable selection. *[TOUR L145–145]* — evidence: DERIV §A planned
- Policy axes: owner/lifetime (view identity, route, tab, account, document); authority (browser, server, peer quorum, external); residence (memory, URL, cookie, IndexedDB/OPFS, edge, database); durability (ephemeral, reload, offline, retained); replication (none, server fan-out, multi-device, p2p); consistency (authoritative replace, CAS, op rebase, CRDT, manual conflict); disclosure (public, principal, capability, field-level redaction). *[WEB L210–220]* — evidence: none in std-ui
- Compositional laws examples: browser authority cannot reach server secrets; durable replication needs a conflict law; capability disclosure needs a live witness at its authority boundary; a cache may not outlive its disclosure grant. *[WEB L244–248]* — evidence: none
- A move is a semantic diff: `.view → .url` preserves meaning, automatic; `.device → .server` changes authority/disclosure/latency, build prints the diff and asks for approval; unsafe moves refuse. *[WEB L249–251]* — evidence: none
- The logical tab (`.tab`): survives reload and BFCache in the same top-level browsing context; not shared with independently opened tabs; a cloned tab FORKS identity on activation; no crash-recovery guarantee (use `.device`); implemented with session storage + generated tab id + clone detection; a target that cannot provide it refuses or takes a declared fallback. *[WEB L252–261]* — evidence: UI.md §10 renames to `.window` with contract unchanged; not built
- `.device` = origin storage in one browser profile (partitioned, evictable); offline scope plainly: `.device` + drafts + queued actions cover offline CAPTURE under server authority, NOT multi-master/local-first (that is `.replicated`, rung 6). *[WEB L262–266]* — evidence: OBS "Out of scope: the device replica (.59.14) and offline writes"
- Per-profile laws: `.url` → type has a URL format; `.room`, `.replicated` → plain data; `.server` reads are pause points and fail → `?`; `.replicated` → only catalogue types or custom type discharging §4.5. *[WEB L267–271]* — evidence: none
- Cookie mirroring is not neutral: render-critical `.device` state may mirror to a cookie only with a disclosure class; then shows a network/cache diff (sent every request, cacheability changes, tight size budget); default: a placeholder. *[WEB L276–279]* — evidence: none
- `.room` adds a relay to the deploy, `explain` shows it; SwiftUI arrived at profiles too as three wrappers (`@AppStorage`, `@SceneStorage`, `@Query`) — one `at` unifies the spelling, axes keep the meaning. *[WEB L280–283]* — evidence: none
- Presence is not truth: `.room` values expire and may drop; using presence (cursors, typing, online) as a durable fact is refused (§16). *[WEB L285–288]* — evidence: none
- Replication: a merge law over two records does NOT make a record a CRDT (deletion, list order, causality, tombstone GC, uniqueness, schema evolution, permissions, app invariants remain); `at .replicated` is SPECIFIED, RESERVED, ordinary programs get a precise diagnostic until rung 6; an experimental std package may exercise it earlier, production syntax does not outrun the proof system; first a catalogue of PROVED replicated types (register, counter, set, sequence, map-of-T) + composition rules + discharge vocabulary. *[WEB L290–299]* — evidence: grep `replicated` empty
- Custom replication is an advanced interface with obligations: operation semantics, causal metadata, invariant preservation, migration, GC; "proven" names its receipt (exhaustive finite check · property-tested · SMT · proof-carrying library); an unknown answers `Unproven`, never "probably". *[WEB L300–303]* — evidence: none
- RESET vs PRESERVE both right (`/product/1 → /product/2` resetting `qty` right if page-local, wrong if really a cart draft → draft `.tab`); two presentations of one model (list card, detail pane) do NOT share view state — call sites are roles; shared domain truth shared by observation; shared PRESENTATION state declared (provided cell or draft), never an identity coincidence. *[WEB L451–457]* — evidence: sites-as-roles BUILT (sites.av); rest design
- T7 resolved: `.replicated` not in v1 — no v1 case justifies a counterfeit convergence guarantee; `.device` + drafts + queued actions cover offline capture under server authority, not multi-master/local-first (say so plainly); design and reserve the profile now so other axes don't foreclose it; build proved catalogue + discharge vocabulary before ordinary programs may use `at .replicated`; user-defined replication only after the catalogue shows the obligation shape; reserved with precise diagnostic until rung 6; experimental std package may exercise earlier. *[WEB L1563–1584]* — evidence: none in tree
- Provider rules: missing provider is a COMPILE error (lexical, never a runtime stack); nearest wins; a provider must be stable across renders (React `useMemo` footgun); reads flow through the read set; NO GLOBAL STORE — app state is the root's `state` or a `@model`, everything else parameters or providers. (Module-level `state` exists: tests/module_state.) *[COMPILED L115–120]* — evidence: std-ui/src/tests/module_state; no provide
- Effects borrow Effect-TS's SEMANTICS not surface: `Effect<A, E, R>` is a description → effects are data executed by the runtime; typed errors → `Result<T, E>` with exhaustive `catch`; `R` requirements statically checked → `provide`/`env` ("needs a Database" is a compile error). Partial analogue: `Want` data asks realized by the target. *[COMPILED L122–128]* — evidence: std-ui/src/app/app.av `enum Want`
- Scopes/finalizers: `defer` becomes an effect's release, subscriptions clean up; combinators as data (`retry`, `timeout`, `race`, `parallel`) described, testable, inspectable; skip the ceremony — keep meaning small: description, typed errors, typed requirements, resource safety. *[COMPILED L129–133]* — evidence: none found
- Derived/memoized state (`derived visible = cards.filter(...)`, part of the read set). CLAUDE.md subset: `derived x = …` is "expected BREAK" — recompute in a fn. *[COMPILED L135–137]* — evidence: CLAUDE.md "The subset today"
- Speculative actions + receipts (the authority design — beat React rather than match it); inspector and time travel (free: state, msgs, effects are data); a11y invariants as compile checks (built partly: image `check()`). *[COMPILED L142–144]* — evidence: components/image.av `fn check()`; no time-travel found
- Break: one dataset, two sibling panes — no common owner; hatch: lift to nearest common ancestor or pass a shared `Cell`; the compiler cannot choose ownership. *[COMPILED L152–152]* — evidence: doc
- Break: rich effect lifecycles (cancel, retry, cleanup, concurrency); hatch: typed requirements + scopes + a SMALL combinator set, resist growth. Loop owners cancel a left node's work (built partly). *[COMPILED L154–154]* — evidence: app/app.av ("what a node that has left asked is dropped")
- Break: server/client split (authority, offline, rooms) — two copies of state at different latencies; data makes it possible (specs, receipts, merge), not automatic. *[COMPILED L157–157]* — evidence: none
- C. Undo/redo and optimistic updates from the message log: deletes undo/redo stacks, hand-written inverses, optimistic copy + rollback. *[DERIV L174–177]* — evidence: none

- Law 3: the app keeps hearing events; the next turn that runs clears the failure and paints without the notice. *[ARCH L256–257]* — evidence: tests/contained
- Law 4: a failure mid-paint leaves the last page shown — walk, diff, then send, so the patch stream is never half-applied; the notice is a paint of its own from current state. *[ARCH L258–261]* — evidence: tests/contained_roots

- Law 2: the instance that stands is the first built there; an instance is built fresh each paint; the walk keeps the first one at a standing and a later one hands it its PROPS (a copy shares its `state` cells); a handler is a closure over `self`, holding the kept cells. *[ARCH L447–451]* — evidence: tree/standing.av
- Law 3: a seed is read once — a state member's instance value (`n: 10` or default) seeds the place when the instance first stands; later values are not read; a new key is a new instance. *[ARCH L452–455]* — evidence: tests/instance_life
- Law 4: a paint offers what stood, once — each reached standing is opened, the instance and standings offered, what the paint does not take is gone; an instance lives exactly while a paint reaches it — no list of the living, no sweep; keyed rows keep standing through reorder; a row a filter hides has LEFT and its state leaves. *[ARCH L456–461]* — evidence: tree/standing.av; tests/instance_life
- Law 7: the app owns what stands — kept instance held by its standing, behind a fn, under the app's root; two screens over one view share nothing; a dropped screen takes its instances; beside each composition its derive mints one `once fn` — only the typed SEAT an instance crosses, holding nothing between calls; its name and the body-method's are unwritable by programs, so a component's own `fn`s take any name; `App.held()` counts what stands. *[ARCH L468–476]* — evidence: tree/standing.av:284 `fn held()`
- Law 8: a write during the walk is a write — it lands, the rest of the paint reads it, no paint follows from it (only the door paints); a place is a `Cell`; pinned (`tests/instance_life`). *[ARCH L477–480]* — evidence: std-ui/src/tests/instance_life
- Law 9: an unkeyed instance stands at its site — two instances at two places never trade state (even two literals written alike; a site is file + content + turn among like literals); instances one site made with no key, or sharing a key, are told apart by order, and the walk SAYS so: `App.warnings()` after each paint and once on the error stream: "`counter` keeps state, and here its instances are told apart by their order alone — they share a key, or one site made them and none wears one: give each its own `key`". *[ARCH L481–493]* — evidence: tree/standing.av:112 `fn warnings()`

- The forced shape: `view(state) -> tree` pure (tree is DATA) and `update(state, msg) -> (state, effects)` pure (msg and effects DATA), the compiler generating everything between. Built loop is one door `turn` running closures (`Act = fn() -> bool`) over mutable state cells, with `Want` as data asks. *[COMPILED L22–27]* — evidence: std-ui/src/app/app.av `enum Msg { Turn(act: Act …) … }`, `enum Want`
- Decision 1: the tree is data — `view(state) -> tree` pure, comparable, serializable, inspectable, no closures, no hidden vdom nodes. Overridden by closure handlers (EVENTS Part 6, COMPONENT_EXPANSION L255). *[DERIV L25–31]* — evidence: docs/2026_10_18_EVENTS.md; tree/node.av `fn fire`
- Why free: messages are content-addressed DATA and state snapshotable, so `undo` = replay to previous state, `redo` = replay forward, optimistic = speculative state before the receipt rolled back on failure; `button "Undo" { on click { undo() } }` with nothing hand-written. Premise (messages as data) overridden by closure handlers. *[DERIV L179–190]* — evidence: app/app.av `Msg.Turn(act: Act)`
- Depends on: generated `Msg` variants with typed payloads + one exhaustive `update` (avra-xubk.2.1) — the log IS that stream, this rides it; board's `enum Msg` payload-free; snapshot/replay of `state` (@model/authority avra-8sb5.59.12; ORM's "DB as a versioned value": agent self-undo, audit history); a `log`/`history` API. Generated Msg never built. *[DERIV L198–209]* — evidence: app/app.av
- "Strongest argument for the generated-message design": once messages are data and sites content-addressed, time travel, undo, redo, audit, optimistic updates are one artifact; storing fns in the tree (rejected 2a) can never replay. The tree went the fn-field way. *[DERIV L211–215]* — evidence: EVENTS Part 6

- Ask: `state` parameters `fn f(state n: int)`. *[UI L682–682]* — evidence: SeatMark.state in features/seat_builder.av

- Rule: an INSTANCE's head is its props, `word(props) { body }`; one positional prop drops the parens: `toggle(done, title)` · `text "Hi"` · `card_detail(c)`; a view is instanced like any component. *[UI L697–697]* — evidence: one bare head value BUILT (`text "Hi"`, `counter "left"`); ARCH Open: an instance takes ONE head value (avra-8sb5.11.331); a module's own word before `(` is a call, refused
- Rule: BODY = HOW: children, look (`key: value`), behaviour (`on event { }`); bare statements run on the primary event: `button("Delete") { b.delete() }`. *[UI L699–699]* — evidence: settings + `on press` BUILT; bare statements via `@default` (compiler BUILT, std-ui not marked)
- Retired from v1: `when` (clashed with the `when` expression), `on .ios`, settings carrying meaning (`map { center: … }`), `hotkey` blocks (a `key:` prop on the control; menus and palettes derive from it), `catalogue` (the app's views are the catalogue), `task` for sync work. *[UI L706–709]* — evidence: `when` expr exists (features/when_expr); hotkey-as-`key:`-prop itself OVERRIDDEN by EVENTS ruling 1: a shortcut is a `key` sub-component (std-ui/components/key.av)
- Until L4 the landed forms are: `toggle title { value: done }`, `on press { }`, `impl Shows for T`, `hover { }` modifier blocks. *[UI L775–776]* — evidence: `on press` and `Shows` BUILT; hover blocks OVERRIDDEN by StateStyle data (UI_LIBRARY §4a); `toggle` is checkbox/switch
- Props in the head: `button("Buy", kind: .Primary)`; the body keeps children and code slots. *[UI L873–874]* — evidence: one head value + block settings (ARCH Open .331); no `kind`
- C3 Props in the head `button("Buy", kind: .Primary)` (wanted by every component with a head: `@std/cli`, `@std/http` routes, `deploy`) — OPEN, sugar, the L4 row. *[UI L918–918]* — evidence: one head value only (ARCH Open avra-8sb5.11.331)
- The three sugars (flat keys, chaining, value spread) are ROADMAP asks named by wanting site; the substrate ships first so sugar never becomes a second mechanism. *[LIBRARY L339–342]* — evidence: substrate shipped; sugars absent
- Open 6: slots and templates — the L4 sugar (`@template`, default slot, builder syntax); design when a real component wants it. *[LIBRARY L585–586]* — evidence: `@default` event slot BUILT (features/components/check.av); `@template` absent

- What blocks `files "/" { root: "public" }` as a route line: a component that expands to code takes no `key: value` settings, and a component's head takes one argument (avra-8sb5.11 survey). #### language-change — BUILT (19)

- C1 Field spread in `type` and `component`: `..Common` (wanted beyond UI by HTTP options, DB configs, CLI flags) — landed #59 (`..R`). *[UI L912–916]* — evidence: std-ui/style/box.av `..Stand`; `..Box` in components
- C2 Generic traits: `trait Realize<T>`, `impl Realize<text> for Html` (wanted by `From<T>`, `Encode<Json>`, drivers, codecs) — landed #59. *[UI L917–917]* — evidence: std-avrac features/impls/tests/generic_trait_impl; packages/std-convert/src/convert.av `trait From<T>`
- C2 substrate complete: #67 (dispatch on a `dyn` argument, completeness law, defaults in generic traits), #79 (a bound names a generic trait at its args, `fn f<R: Realize<Text>>`), #87 (generic trait static members — `From<T>` in `@std/convert`). *[UI L920–923]* — evidence: features/impls/tests/dyn_dispatch, generic_builtin_trait; std-convert
- Proposed (owner to confirm): no `html { }` sublanguage now — views are Avra components, HTML is an OUTPUT, hand-written HTML would bypass the interaction contract; content arrives as files (`embed("post.md")`, `embed("legacy.html")`) parsed at build into the same semantic tree, no new lexer mode; an inline `html { }` (lexer mode, typed context-escaped holes, explicit `trusted`) waits for a real wanting site. *[WEB L2115–2122]* — evidence: no `html {}` sublanguage in std-ui (views are components); embed-into-tree not built
- Why `collect` is core and not a component: "does this statement leave a value?" is a TYPING fact; a component expands at parse and cannot tell `o.id` from `println(…)`. *[EXPANSION L242–244]* — evidence: features/collects/check.av
- `from` NEEDS NO SYNTAX: `production with { region: "eu" }`; reserving `from` would break ~166 names. (REJECTED: a `from` keyword.) *[EXPANSION L285–287]* — evidence: §9 table L422
- `alias Name<T> = <type>` — a shorthand, same type (F2121); `type` over a fn shape is opaque at seats like any name; `Handler` in `@std/http`. *[EXPANSION L568–570]* — evidence: std-ui/src/app/app.av `export alias Act = fn() -> bool`
- A component's body: a component declaration takes statements beside fields and `fn`s — child instances, `if/for/match`, `let`, a call answering a view — never its own declaration; the compiler folds them into one quote; nothing walks a body where written. *[ARCH L497–503]* — evidence: features/components/mod.av `body:stmt`
- `@composes(Carried)` (`@std/meta`) on a trait: a component with a body is one of these; derived over that trait as a written `@derive` would be — `derive` receives the body as `Type.body` and splices it — and it CARRIES the named record as if spread; so the compiler names no library: `@std/ui` writes `@composes(Stand) export trait View`; `key:` and the site are fields of every composition. *[ARCH L504–511]* — evidence: std-meta/src/meta.av:362; std-ui/src/tree/view.av:70 `@composes(Stand)`
- Which trait: the nearest files declaring a `@composes` trait walking outward from the component's file (its `use` names' files, its module's files, then theirs); `text`-made body reaches `View` through `text`'s file; composition-only body through theirs with no `use` of the library; only files a build reads, never a package's tests; two libraries each compose their own; none in reach refused, two in the nearest ring refused. *[ARCH L512–521]* — evidence: features/components (type.component_body)
- A generated impl names its trait where the name was WRITTEN: `impl View for counter` is the library's text, `View` read in the library's file whatever the component file imports; no import bound into the user's file; what lands is the impl and the law-7 seat under unwritable names. *[ARCH L522–527]* — evidence: features/components
- A component is instanced where it is declared: a module's own component word is reserved nowhere; it opens an instance before a head value (`counter "a"`, `counter id`, `counter self.name`) anywhere, or before a name at statement start; its word before `{` stays the record literal (`header {}`); the parse knows the module's words by name, so an instance stands as a hole and the declaration keeps its shape. *[ARCH L528–535]* — evidence: features/components/mod.av
- A statement that fails keeps the shape around it: recovery skips the block the failed statement opened, not only its line, so the enclosing declaration still closes. *[ARCH L536–538]* — evidence: grammar recovery (UNKNOWN exact symbol)
- An instance's head keeps its brace: after a component word, a name then a brace is head + block (`counter id { key: id }`); a record-literal head followed by a block stands in parens (`tally (Tally { … }) { … }`), `fmt` keeps them; a head naming a record is refused (`type.component_head`); other heads (`if`, `while`, `for`, `match`, `if let`, `let … else`) take a literal bare; a module's own word before `(` is a call, refused with "bind the value first". *[ARCH L539–547]* — evidence: components/mod.av "type.component_head"; CLAUDE.md subset line on `tally (Span …)`
- `component C { state n: T = v }` is a field, as a record's is. *[ARCH L548–548]* — evidence: forms/form_inputs.av `state typed: Map<string, string> = {}`
- A WRITE THROUGH A `state` FIELD ASKS NOTHING OF ITS ROOT: the cell takes it, so `self.n = …` in a handler and `c.n = …` on a parameter run; `self.plain = …` keeps `resolve.immutable`'s words; the root's verdict is spoken at typing; such a write marks no receiver written. *[ARCH L549–553]* — evidence: tests/rows_test `state mine`; features/mutation
- `@std/meta` gains `Field.state`, `Type.body`, `composes`. *[ARCH L554–554]* — evidence: std-meta/src/meta.av:362
- `@std/meta`'s `Site`: a field's type is its meaning — a record literal leaving a `Site` field unset holds its own site (file, own content, turn among like literals, folded), owing no default; one constant per literal; content-addressed so a line above moves nothing, and two literals written alike are still two. *[ARCH L555–560]* — evidence: std-meta/src/meta.av:200-204
- Compiler learns the `asset` declaration: a path literal read as a build input (octets and dir names), settled at check — one word for every kind, missing file located. PROBED then: `asset logo = "…"` was `parse.expected`; `embed` was text-only (a PNG trapped "is not UTF-8 — byte 0"). Now the asset statement parses and reads octets. *[ASSETS L84–88]* — evidence: features/asset/mod.av

- Alternatives table (one image / icon folder / font+licence / dark variant): A. a word per kind (`picture logo { source: … }`, `icons glyph { source: … }`, `typeface inter { source, license }`, a `variant {}` child); B. one word one file (`asset logo = "art/logo.svg"`, `asset glyph = "art/icons"`, `asset inter = "…ttf"`, `{ dark: … }`); C. a literal at the use (`image asset"art/logo.svg"`, `.Bundled(asset"…ttf")`, nowhere for a variant); D. convention (`image assets.logo`, `icon assets.icons.close`, sidecar file); PICKED B over a folder (0 tokens per file, licence read from the font, 1 line for a variant). *[ASSETS L47–55]* — evidence: doc §0
- Why not D: saves four tokens once per package but costs a name from nowhere in every file, a resolver special rule, no ordinary `export`; `avra new app` writes the line. *[ASSETS L58–58]* — evidence: doc
- Why not C: a variant, licence and mirror list have no home; a folder has no members. *[ASSETS L59–59]* — evidence: doc
- Why not A: three words to learn for a fact the bytes already state. *[ASSETS L60–60]* — evidence: doc
- Not an annotation (an annotation's effect is its answer type, and `at` is already the language's word for a home); not the manifest (a second place for a fact the app declaration owns). *[ASSETS L177–177]* — evidence: doc
- Rejected: a word per kind, a literal at the use, a silent `assets/` convention (§0). *[ASSETS L241–243]* — evidence: doc

- Table: a non-package directory → serves its files; a package whose program mounts a page → builds, serves, watches, rebuilds, tells the page; a package with no program → refuses (a library, and what a page is); a program mounting no page → refuses (`avra run` runs it); a file → refuses. *[ARCH L620–626]* — evidence: commands/dev.av

- The watch set is the build's own inputs (`Workspace.build_inputs`: closure sources, manifests, toolchain), re-asked every turn so new files are seen; polled every 250 ms; a file is its stamp or — too young — its text, so a no-change save builds nothing; what is remembered is what a build STARTED from; nothing watched where nothing rebuilds. *[ARCH L673–679]* — evidence: commands/dev.av
- The page is told over an event stream (`/@dev/events`, `@std/http`'s `sse.av`): `built` says the standing module's number (count of builds that made another module), `failed` says the build's words; a stream not a socket (server only tells, browser reopens itself, late joiner hears current state first). *[ARCH L680–686]* — evidence: tests/dev_test.av:265 `/@dev/events`

- The physics: a screen is a function of state; state lives in places separated by latency (browser ~0 ms, server 50–300 ms, build time = already past); shared truth has an authority, everyone else only speculates; HTML is free to run, every byte of script costs. *[WEB L32–37]* — evidence: COMPILED "five facts" #1 "A UI is a function of state" (BUILT as view fn); latency/authority placement not built

- §9 Ten-year expectations mapped to mechanisms: agents operate every app (actions + semantic tree); one codebase native everywhere no VM (LLVM per target + op stream + native realizers); offline by default (device replica); true privacy labels (reach + `@personal`); every bug reproducible (segment-commit turns + receipts → traces); UI you can ask "why" (`explain` + inspector on device); future form factors (semantic-first → templates, voice, spatial). *[UI L577–591]* — evidence: only the turn loop, MCP door, wasm/native compile exist

- Prior-art take/leave table: Svelte/Solid (take compile-time reactivity, direct DOM patches; leave runtime signal APIs); Qwik (resumability, serializability as constitution; leave `$` ceremony); Astro (zero-JS default, islands; leave hand-marked islands); Remix (forms without JS, loaders/actions; leave route files as truth); LiveView/HTMX (server truth, no API layer; leave round trip per keystroke); Convex/Zero/Replicache/Linear (live queries, speculation + server rebase; leave a separate backend product); Elm (pure view, no runtime exceptions; leave message boilerplate); StyleX/Tailwind (typed styles, atomic CSS, tokens; leave class-name strings); SwiftUI (modifiers+environment, identity, `.task`, `withAnimation`, preferences, focus/nav as state; leave per §19); React (`useTransition`/`useDeferredValue`, portals, `key`; leave hooks rules, dependency arrays). *[WEB L79–94]* — evidence: COMPILED per-site patches; std-ui/src/style/tokens.av typed tokens; keyed list tests/keyed_list; Elm message model OVERRIDDEN by ARCH (handler is a closure)

- Reference semantics first: every optimization proved OBSERVATIONALLY EQUIVALENT to the slow model; fallbacks change COST never MEANING. *[WEB L120–123]* — evidence: COMPILED "Safety: the tree is the oracle" keeps the tree as reference semantics; equivalence proofs not built

- The wire (version 12): a frame is the version byte then one record per patch (`realize/dom/wire.av` the one definition, `wire.gen.js` written from it); a number is 4 LE bytes unless *varint* (7-bit groups, low first, high bit continues: 1 byte <128, 2 <16,384, 3 <2,097,152); an id is the varint of one more than its number (zero = none); a text is byte length then bytes. - Seam 1, DIFF LIVES IN AVRA: a node lowers to target elements (`realize/html/element.av`, the one place knowing a control sits under its label); two element trees matched by id (`realize/dom/patch.av`); host applies patches: create, create text, place (after a sibling; one op inserts and moves), remove, set attribute, drop attribute, set property, set text, listen, unlisten, style, call. A `call` asks an element for its own verb said last in its frame: a DOOR (dialog `open` vs `showModal` with focus inside) — the attribute is never sent; its arrival/leaving are calls. *[ARCH L34–45]* — evidence: realize/dom/patch.av `Patch.Call(id, d.enter)`

- A first paint is a diff against nothing; an unchanged page is no patch (the diff is a stateless fold: old page and new in, patches and numbered page out). THE PAGE IS THE TREE FOR WHAT THE TREE NAMES, ELSE THE HOST'S: a property written only when the program draws other than it knows the page holds (typed field never written back); a door too — a user-dismissed dialog is closed by the page before the program hears, not closed again, reshown if the program still says open. *[ARCH L55–64]* — evidence: realize/dom/patch.av `stands_open`
- Seam 7, `mount(view)` IS THE APP: no app writes the host seam; the page's exports and one import are the web module's own (`web/web.av`); the program's statements are its start. *[ARCH L133–137]* — evidence: std-ui/src/web/web.av
- Said once: a seat's event facts are the same for every element, so a `define` precedes the first `listen` needing it; `Live` remembers what it defined (`told`); a memoryless frame is self-contained; the host refuses a `listen` no `define` named. *[ARCH L211–216]* — evidence: realize/dom/live.av `told: Cell<List<int>>`
- Reconnecting: a new connection starts from an empty page, so `Live.reconnected()` forgets page, sheet, bindings and definitions together; next paint says all; nothing in production reconnects yet; the verb is the door a server-side page must use; `page_test` holds it. *[ARCH L222–227]* — evidence: realize/dom/live.av:295-304
- Listening is one capturing listener per event word on the mount; host keeps (element, seat → says) as data and finds hearers walking up from the target; a version mismatch is the existing refusal — a cached page of another version is told, never misapplied. *[ARCH L227–231]* — evidence: runtime/dom/bootstrap.js; wire.av:48
- What the page keeps (seam 1's law): a property is written only when the value drawn — rest, on the paint that stops naming it — is not the last value the program drew or heard there; a never-named property is never written. *[ARCH L294–299]* — evidence: realize/dom/patch.av
- A page holds more than any tree says: typed text, caret, selection, focus, scroll, open `<details>`, video position, canvas pixels, running animation, whatever a future host keeps — listed nowhere; it survives because of two things the diff does NOT do. *[ARCH L301–305]* — evidence: patch.av
- Rule 1: it writes no name the program does not draw; a property is the PROGRAM's while its tree names it, else the HOST's; naming is writing it at the instance (`field "Name" {}` names nothing, `field "Name" { value: draft }` names one, empty or not; optional `@attr` gives its attribute only when given; `checked: false` names the tick as much as `true`). *[ARCH L307–313]* — evidence: patch.av; components/field.av
- Cases: named and drawn as known → nothing said (repaint never reaches a field mid-typing); named and drawn otherwise → written (known = what a handler heard since last paint else what that paint drew; typing taken up writes nothing; `value: ""` after send clears; a mask/limit/refusal gets its value); no longer named → drawn at rest once, then the host's; newly named → written; never named → never written; a new element stands at rest, sent only what is off rest. *[ARCH L314–330]* — evidence: patch.av; realize/dom/tests
- Rule 2: it never remakes a node that stays: keyed row that moves is CARRIED (`place`); changed attribute/property/handler/class set on the standing element; changed words rewrite their text run, element stands; an element already in place is untouched. *[ARCH L331–339]* — evidence: patch.av
- THE CORNER: program draws `checked: false` every paint, user ticks — with no handler the tick stands until another value is drawn; with a handler that hears the tick, drawing `false` unticks, drawing `true` says nothing; a box with no `checked` written is the host's. *[ARCH L341–346]* — evidence: realize/dom/tests
- Made again (another node): primitive changed to another element kind or heading level; words ↔ element at one seat; its key; its parent (node or composition instance carried elsewhere; instance state follows); a root switching between one node and several (the several stand under a column). *[ARCH L348–356]* — evidence: patch.av
- The host's half (`runtime/dom/bootstrap.js`): a property written only when it differs from the element's; carried by `moveBefore` where available (drops nothing); otherwise remove and re-insert with focus and caret handed back (scroll, video place, animation lost only there). *[ARCH L363–369]* — evidence: runtime/dom/bootstrap.js:300-304
- Its key shows on the first node it draws (page's `data-key`) where that node wears none of its own. *[ARCH L429–431]* — evidence: realize/html/element.av (data-key) — UNKNOWN exact symbol
- Serving a directory is `@std/http`'s own (`files.av`'s `directory`: path-safe, validated, ranged, typed by name — `application/wasm`); smallest program: `server site { port: 8080  routes: directory("/", Files { root: "public" }) }` and `match site.run() { .Ok(n) -> "served ${n}", .Err(e) -> e.describe().message }`. *[ARCH L641–655]* — evidence: packages/std-http/src/files.av (UNKNOWN exact line)

#### data/models — BUILT (6)

- Order rationale: a cache's correctness IS its invalidation, affordable only as computed from a query's reads, so no cache before that; interim: a page's read is `fetched(held, request, parse)`; a GET observed under path+query, shared by every view asking it, kept after its last reader leaves, asked again when a write's answer names a table it read; `parse` is pure (no state assignment, a refresh reruns it). *[WEB L624–632]* — evidence: std-ui/src/app/asks.av `held` doc comment; tests/observed, observed_writes, observed_reask, observed_unmount
- S6 DECIDED WITH OWNER (2026-09-24): models are types, not components. A component instance is always a VALUE; a TYPE is declared with `type`; behaviour comes from `@derive`. Example `@model type User = { @unique email: string  name: string }`. *[EXPANSION L352–363]* — evidence: std-crud tests `@model`; packages/std-db
- `@model` is a library ANNOTATION FN bundling derives (`Model.derive(t).concat(Json.derive(t))`, like SwiftData `@Model`); a trait's `derive` is `static fn derive(t: Type) -> List<Directive>` with optional `marks`, held at declaration (F2134) and each `@derive` (F2072, F2066); unclaimed mark F2092; generator sees fields, spelled types, marks, `///`, annotations; output typed with the program so models can name each other (joins); every model at once via `collect models = impl Model in closure` (avra-8sb5.28). Built form: `collect models: List<Found> = @model in closure as Found {…} by it.name`. *[EXPANSION L365–373]* — evidence: packages/std-meta/src/meta.av:808; packages/std-db/src/models.av:20
- Law 5: state that must outlive a hide is the MODEL's — a `state` field of the model's own record, or module state; lives as long as the row, no paint frees it. *[ARCH L462–464]* — evidence: tests/module_state
- Law 7: bytes the build has are an asset; a name for bytes someone else has is a `Url`; `remote(url, width, height)` is the spelled exception — sized by author, never transformed, shipped or precached. *[ASSETS L72–72]* — evidence: std-assets/src/assets.av:31 `fn remote(url: Url, width: int, height: int) -> Picture?`
- Runtime value: a handle is a settled const (static data, no alloc, no I/O); `Attribute.Src(url: Url)` becomes `Attribute.Picture(Picture)` with `Picture = .Bundled(name, width, height, tone, media, octets) | .Remote(url, width, height)`; a bundled picture is named by CONTENT (first 128 bits of BLAKE3 of its octets, hex, plus the media type's extension), never by path, so two builds agree by name and one name is one set of octets; recipe settings join the name when transforms land. ### perf (46)

#### perf — PLANNED (30)

- Derived work runs on the cores by the fork law (BEND §4); `parallel { }` becomes a Worker pool on web, threads on native. *[UI L440–441]* — evidence: none found in std-ui
- Frame law: a main-thread handler carries `@cost(steps: ms(8))` by default, 120 Hz targets `ms(4)`. *[UI L442–443]* — evidence: no `@cost` in packages
- Scoreboard (added to WEB_UI §20.2): Boards on each native target vs SwiftUI, Compose, RN and Flutter builds — cold start, resident memory, binary size, frame time p99, input-to-paint; published as receipts, "frontier not score". *[UI L648–653]* — evidence: none found (tools/ui-board/listen_bench is a web listener bench only)
- Budgets: per-frame handler `@cost`, byte budget per target, layout node count; a build reports its worst case. *[LIBRARY L484–485]* — evidence: none found
- Subscription budgets: per-node signals cheap to update/expensive to hold, per-view the reverse — measure, never assume. *[LIBRARY L639–640]* — evidence: none found
- Runtime tracking (reads through a fn value, `dyn`, a runtime key) is a PERFORMANCE fallback with identical behaviour; `explain` lists fallback sites; pure derived reads ELIGIBLE for dependency-tracked caching under explicit semantics (equality, error, lifetime); divergence from FULL_SPEC 29.3: runtime `Signal<T>` is the fallback, not the default. *[WEB L352–358]* — evidence: no `Signal<` in tree; fallback listing not found
- Queries: independent reads start together; keyed fan-out batched; every remaining sequential dependency visible in `explain` and charged to latency; avoidable N+1 diagnosed; link hover prefetches the target's independent reads; streaming SSR on fibers, slow queries stream out of order. *[WEB L590–595]* — evidence: grep prefetch empty; dedupe BUILT (shared GET observation, asks.av)
- Caching as a privacy + freshness algebra: the most permissive policy consistent with declared constraints, computed from disclosure class and principal dependence, query/version dependencies, request variance (locale, negotiation, experiments), freshness requirement and staleness permission, invalidation reach, legal/geographic storage constraints. *[WEB L597–605]* — evidence: OBS §3 rules (key, freshness, auth/logout) is the successor design; disclosure-class algebra not built
- Relation-write invalidation is the safe COARSE fallback; predicate-indexed selective purge at scale is RESEARCH (measure fan-out, churn, backpressure, reconnect storms). *[WEB L607–609]* — evidence: coarse table-named invalidation BUILT (asks.av: asked again "when a write's answer names a table it read", tests/observed_writes); selective purge not built
- Threads: `parallel { }` → Worker pool; `SharedArrayBuffer` needs COOP/COEP which break non-opted-in embeds, trade shown; debug: DWARF in WASM, one trace id client → server. *[WEB L873–875]* — evidence: none found
- Break: 10k-row tables, canvas, games at 60fps — tree-diff is wrong for massive dynamic content; hatch: an IMPERATIVE TARGET consuming the same regions. *[COMPILED L146–150]* — evidence: none
- Break: pointer move/drag/scroll — msg→update→view→frame too slow per move; hatch: the HOT CHANNEL, high-frequency events write transforms directly, batched to frames — a LOWERING, not a user escape. *[COMPILED L151–151]* — evidence: none
- Break: `dyn`/polymorphic subtrees make the read set coarse; accept over-invalidation, measure before specializing. *[COMPILED L155–155]* — evidence: doc
- The compiler animates THE CHANGE: read set + previous tree as data say exactly what changed, so choose transition per delta — value change on bound leaf → animate the property; list reorder → animate moves (keyed reconciler knows key path); insert/remove → enter/exit; subtree swap → animate the swap's root. *[DERIV L410–420]* — evidence: none
- Gap 1 Virtualization (`.35`): a 10,000-row list builds, walks and diffs 10,000 nodes every paint; depends on L1 scroll + `.36` text metrics + keyed list (#248 landed). *[DERIV L746–748]* — evidence: grep virtual empty
- Lifting a handler into `(site, captures)` stays open as a compiler LOWERING indistinguishable from the reference. *[ARCH L16–17]* — evidence: none
- Law 10: only what is reached ships, at the sizes it is drawn; an unreached file costs zero bytes on every target. *[ASSETS L75–75]* — evidence: no reach-based asset table found
- Reach per member of a settled record — an unused icon in a 2,000-icon folder costs nothing; today reach is per declaration (ASSUMED). *[ASSETS L91–91]* — evidence: none
- A collection of only what is reached (`collect … reached`) — each target's table holds exactly what ships. *[ASSETS L92–92]* — evidence: none
- Use-site facts: the settled box of every node that draws a handle — only drawn sizes are made. *[ASSETS L95–95]* — evidence: none
- The size rule: the compiler moves an asset between `.inline` and a file by itself (deployment unchanged), never between `.program` and `.bundle` (changes what must be deployed); over the `.program` budget is an error whose help writes the `at`. *[ASSETS L178–178]* — evidence: none
- Fonts in numbers: variable Inter ~850 KB TTF (~340 KB WOFF2), ~2,900 glyphs; a CJK face 10–20 MB, 65,000 glyphs; a page of English shows 100–200 glyphs — tens of KB. *[ASSETS L181–183]* — evidence: doc (approximate, re-measure)

#### perf — PARTIAL (7)

- §20 The patch loop is NOT a VDOM: the read set is compiler-known and identity is `OriginId`, so a static node is addressed, not matched, and one changed expression emits one op; fine-grained where reads are provable, coarse across a `dyn` boundary. *[LIBRARY L625–630]* — evidence: ARCH: Avra diff matches elements by id/step each paint (realize/dom/patch.av); read sets exist (std-avrac features/read_set.av) but per-site compiled patches (COMPILED) are not the shipped loop
- Exact patches: each cell has identity+policy; each view fn summarized ("arg 0 `.title` → text hole 3"); summaries compose at call sites (state path → derived fns → holes); a write is a PATH so a PROVEN summary patches exactly the holes reached; conditionals, filtered/reordered lists, cross-node relationships (labels, layout) fall back to subtree reconcile; `tasks.push(t)` compiles to an insert op. *[WEB L333–342]* — evidence: COMPILED "compiler picks the mechanism PER SITE" + std-ui/src/tree/sites.av, sited_text tests; path-summary hole patching not confirmed (UNKNOWN extent)
- Keyed selector: `t.id == selected` per keyed row → 2 rows patch not 10k; identical reads on a page share one subscription and one cache entry; same algebra as the compiler memo kernel and `@std/relation`'s `@query` (per-run read/write frames, std-relation/src/db.av) — one incremental substrate, proved by measurement. *[WEB L347–351]* — evidence: shared observation per key BUILT (std-ui/src/app/asks.av `held`: "A GET is observed under its key and SHARED"); keyed selector/one-substrate UNKNOWN
- The collapse: React/SwiftUI/Flutter pick ONE mechanism (a diff) at runtime for everyone; we know statically what each expression reads, so the compiler picks the mechanism PER SITE. *[COMPILED L71–74]* — evidence: tree/sites.av (compiler-written readings) + realize/dom/patch.av (diff)
- Per-site lowering table: static → hoisted into the binary, frame never mentions it (cost 0); one bool → DIRECT WRITE ("pinned changed → set `checked` on node N", one op); a list → KEYED RECONCILER scoped to the list (O(changed)); imperative region → TARGET CALL `target.draw(region, data)` (O(1)); `dyn`/unknown → conservative tree + reconcile. Built: keyed reconcile in patch.av and direct text readings; static hoisting and imperative regions UNKNOWN/not found. *[COMPILED L85–91]* — evidence: realize/dom/patch.av; tree/sites.av
- ONE analysis feeds all: which state does this expression read? The read set is the input to lowering, not just invalidation. Built per text reading and component `reads: Cells`. *[COMPILED L93–94]* — evidence: tree/sites.av `Reading { cells }`; components/button.av `reads: Cells = []`
- Law 9: checking never transforms — `check` reads headers, only `build` encodes; a file's public name is its KEY (source bytes ⊕ recipe ⊕ codec version), one derivation for cache entry and shipped name. Header-reading built; recipes/encoding not. *[ASSETS L74–74]* — evidence: std-assets (headers only)

#### perf — BUILT (5)

- Expansion is ordinary code, no runtime layer; receipt via `avra ir`. One general change: a `defer` of ONE call answering nothing with scalar-literal/capture args is a direct call at each exit (`Deferral.Direct`, no closure box), making every such defer cheaper; `Deferral.Deadline` was its special case. `server` benchmark via tools/bench on a Sprite (S5). *[EXPANSION L196–203]* — evidence: features/contract.av `enum Deferral { … Direct(call: DirectCall) … }`
- Of the elements a parent keeps, the longest run still in order stands still and only the rest are placed, so a reorder says the fewest moves. *[ARCH L46–48]* — evidence: realize/dom/patch.av:260
- The end of a run is said only for a parent made in the same frame (last child placed is its end); a moved/leaving child makes the parent unknown again so later places name their sibling; earlier-frame parents and the page top stay explicit. *[ARCH L217–220]* — evidence: realize/dom/wire.av place encoding
- Measured on 100,000 buttons under one container (`tools/ui-board/listen_bench`, Firefox): wire 10 78 B/button (7.80 MB), wire 11 61 (6.10 MB), wire 12 23 (2.25 MB); listeners 100,000 → 1. *[ARCH L233–235]* — evidence: tools/ui-board/listen_bench

Cost to the compiler's own build: cold 1972 MB → 2177 MB (30 more files); one-file edit 339 → 637 MB; every `@std/http` export compiled into the compiler (1132 symbols vs 411 for a program) because a native program's exports are roots. *[ARCH L661–668]* — evidence: commands/dev.av, dev_backend.av

### libraries-rules (44)

#### libraries-rules — PLANNED (18)

- Platform packages: `@std.camera.{camera}`, `@std.notifications.{remind}`, `@std.purchases.{buy}`, `@std.mail.{mail}`, `@std.time.{days, mins, Time, tomorrow}`, `@std.ai.{agent, answer}`, `@std.deploy.{deploy, secret}`, `@std.testing.{render, fixture}`. *[TOUR L20–27]* — evidence: packages/std-time and std-testing exist; camera, notifications, purchases, mail, ai, deploy absent
- Deprecation is visible: an old name stays with a migration note until a major version. *[LIBRARY L500–501]* — evidence: none found
- Records and profiles: the RECORD is the truth and advanced escape, the PROFILE the ordinary surface; every public semantic record ships ≥1 canonical profile, a lossless `explain` expansion, semantic-law tests on the record, cold-start authoring tests on the profile; lands only when both gates pass; cold-start gate has no veto over semantic truth; a profile whose expansion loses a policy the record knows is refused. *[WEB L161–172]* — evidence: UI.md §0 keeps "record + profile"; no `@profile` in tree
- Two checks: INTRINSIC compositional laws at the declaration (policy shape); CONTEXTUAL laws at every use (`at .url` needs a URL codec for `T`; `.room(key)` obligations on key and payload); `explain` expands through the ordinary declaration. *[WEB L188–191]* — evidence: none
- "Every law, one table" legend: R = refuses · O = obligation (test/runtime check generated) · W = warns. *[WEB L1043–1045]* — evidence: n/a
- Laws (part 2): a reserved profile is unavailable precisely (R, `at .replicated` before rung 6); placement never widens visibility (R); a placement change is a semantic diff (R); a fallback changes cost never meaning (R); input feels instant (R: keystroke path needing a round trip with no pending state); budgets hold (R: over `js:`/cost budget); render reads no browser-only value (R: `viewport.width`); server code stays on the server (R: `Db`/secret in browser placement); a pause cuts time visibly (R: gesture use, `preventDefault`, atomicity across pause); reach is declared (R). *[WEB L1057–1066]* — evidence: none
- Laws (part 4): dynamic contrast holds (O); target size per profile (R); raw colour names its space and treatment (R: bare hex outside theme); an image has known dimensions (R: layout-shifting image); a UI-thread handler fits its frame (R: 80 ms handler); recovery is designed not implied (R: resumable/offline/SW state with no migration/rejection path); every generated choice names its provenance (R); lost update across a pause (W); runtime-tracking fallback site (W: read through `dyn`); pending/empty/failed left to theme defaults (W); keyboard/AT/zoom/forced-colors behaviour (O: generated interaction traces). *[WEB L1079–1089]* — evidence: image dimensions planned in docs/2026_10_05_UI_ASSETS.md §7 ("What the compiler proves"); none built
- T1 resolved: semantics co-designed with spelling; every public semantic record ships ≥1 canonical profile, lossless `explain` expansion, semantic-law tests on the record and cold-start authoring tests on the profile; lands only when both pass; cold-start gate has no veto over semantic truth (a record may need to stabilise first); a delightful profile is unacceptable if its expansion loses a policy → §3.1. *[WEB L1360–1378]* — evidence: same as L161-172 entry
- T2 resolved: Claude's proposal (profiles the ONLY legal points, one-field overrides, a compatibility table, refuse outside naming nearest) REJECTED as too restrictive — valid policies need coordinated multi-field changes; "meaningless" examples (browser authority + capability disclosure; server fan-out of browser-authored facts) are valid under the right threat model; a hand-kept table ages into framework opinion; consensus: profiles canonical/default, one `with { … }` overrides one or more fields atomically, checked against compositional laws, whole diff printed, nearest profile suggested, profile-less policy is the P8 hatch; P1 protected because ordinary code uses profiles. Follow-on T11. *[WEB L1380–1414]* — evidence: rejected alternative recorded; adopted design unbuilt
- Fetched, content-pinned dependencies (§4). PROBED: `"@std/icons" = "1.0.0"` is `manifest.unsupported_dependency` — only path deps resolve. *[ASSETS L97–97]* — evidence: no avra.lock
- In tree: PNG/JPEG/WebP decode, resample, PNG/WebP encode (`@std/image`, vendored C run by the build); WOFF2 (brotli already vendored — PROBED); delegated, pinned by version and digest: AVIF, Apple's catalog compiler; absent → an error naming the row. *[ASSETS L117–117]* — evidence: no std-image package; std-compress exists
- Options for non-toolchain art: in the toolchain download (every install pays MBs); platform-native first (name map per platform; zero bytes with SF Symbols / system face); a fetched package pinned by the toolchain (HTTPS GET of an archive, `sha256` pin, `avra.lock`, machine cache `~/.avra/cache/<sha256>`; network once); a URL/git dependency by the app (same transport, git needs `git`); copy into the app (`avra add icon rocket`: file in `art/` with origin and digest; never network again). *[ASSETS L124–130]* — evidence: none
- Recommended: native first, fetched fallback — `use @std.icons.{icons}` → `icon icons.share {}`; on Apple an SF Symbol (zero bytes), elsewhere SVG from the fetched `@std/icons` (Apache-licensed), only reached shapes ship. *[ASSETS L132–134]* — evidence: no std-icons
- The toolchain carries the PIN (name → URL + `sha256`), not the bytes — `@std/*` stays the toolchain's; a toolchain version names exactly one set of bytes. *[ASSETS L136–136]* — evidence: none
- This is the first network transport and first lockfile in the tree — the minimum the old registry spec's content-addressed lock needs, no registry service: a static host and a hash. *[ASSETS L140–140]* — evidence: none
- P14 build-time vs run-time: a subsetter runs in the build and leaves nothing in the product (a compiler dependency like LLVM); a shaper on a drawn target is in the product — a runtime, so it must be ours or the platform's. *[ASSETS L198–198]* — evidence: doc
- For the owner 3: fetching starts here — toolchain-held pin, HTTPS + `sha256`, `avra.lock`, `~/.avra/cache`, `--frozen`; recommended minimum, the first step of the package transport — say if it should be its own campaign first. *[ASSETS L264–264]* — evidence: none

#### libraries-rules — PARTIAL (8)

- §19.2 Planned tree: `view.av` (View, Shows); `components/` one file per component (text, heading, image, icon, button, link, toggle, input, picker, column, row, grid, scroll, spacer, divider, region, list, item, table, form, fieldset, dialog, popover, menu, status, canvas, foreign); `style/` (style.av, tokens.av, theme.av); `a11y/` (name.av, roles.av, faults.av); `realize/realizer.av` (trait + walk); `realize/html/` (document, writer, css, per component); `realize/tui/` (layout, per component); `web/screen.av` (Screen over @std/http); `testing/` (transcript.av, snapshot.av). *[UI L885–901]* — evidence: actual: tree/ (view, node, shows, identity…), components/ (25 files), style/, a11y/name.av only, realize/{html,dom,tui}, web/web.av (mount), headless/, place/; no realizer.av, no testing/

- `std-ui_html`, `std-ui_tui` gone (now `realize/html`, `realize/tui`); `std-ui_web` becomes `std-ui_http` (a `Document` from a route); `std-ui_gallery` stands on the one model until the gallery becomes an `avra` command over `testing`. *[UI L907–910]* — evidence: packages list; gallery is still a package (no cli gallery command)
- Bar: extensibility — compose new components from primitives; new targets are new directories; a versioned, closed primitive set. *[LIBRARY L39–40]* — evidence: compositions (ARCH), closed `Prim`, realize/<target> dirs
- Growth crosses, moving refuses: component fields may be appended, a capability row appended; rename/reorder/shrink is a two-step (stop the door refusing, move, restore) — the crossing law. *[LIBRARY L496–499]* — evidence: CLAUDE.md crossing law; wire versioning (v12)
- Laws (part 1): truth copied only as a based branch (R, query result copied into `state`); untrusted text never becomes authority-bearing syntax (R); every interactive object has one operable semantic contract (R: click-only surface, unnamed control, canvas without peer); authority/impact/safeguard/receipt/waiver/mandate laws (R, AUTHORITY §12); speculative UI never impersonates a committed fact (R); a web invocation site discharges its action's safeguard (R: `.destructive` button with no confirm/undo/trash/re-auth); a profile override is checked whole (R); a dotted profile name is unambiguous (R). *[WEB L1047–1056]* — evidence: escaping (html.av) enforces the untrusted-text law at runtime; UI.md §3.3 "server truth is never state"; rest not built
- Laws (part 3): render and derived fns are pure (R); effects form no cycle (R); list binding held across a pause is keyed (R: `tasks[i]` after a pause); stateful/reorderable list child has identity (R); a derived value has no place (R: derived → `State<T>` seat); a provider exists (R: `env T` with no `provide`); a profile's type fits it (R: non-encodable at `.url`, non-plain at `.room`); replicated types proved or discharged (R); presence is not truth (R); browser's state ownership respected (R: overwriting selection/composition/autofill); user preferences outrank decoration (R: forced colors/reduced motion/zoom defeated); every mode fills every role, static contrast holds (R). *[WEB L1067–1078]* — evidence: keyed lists BUILT (std-ui/src/tests/keyed_list, unkeyed_owner); themes per mode (style/theme.av); others not built
- `trait Asset`: a kind claims files by leading bytes, reads a handle at check, projects artifacts at build; kinds collected program-wide; kinds are LIBRARY code (`@std/assets`) so a package can add one (Lottie, glTF, shader). Built as `@std/meta`'s `Provider` with per-kind `provide` (claims + handle); build-time projection not. *[ASSETS L90–90]* — evidence: std-assets/src/assets.av `use @std.meta.{Provider, …}`; tests `Picture.provide("a.png", …)`

- Invocation-site discharge: every button/form/menu item/shortcut invoking an action is an INVOCATION SITE that discharges the safeguard there (confirmation via `ask`, a real undo affordance, trash, re-authentication, typed phrase); an undo toast discharges only when recovery is real; the gallery generates each site's traces (accidental activation, keyboard activation, repeated/bulk, cancellation, recovery). *[WEB L532–539]* — evidence: UI.md §3.4 "Safeguards are discharged by default, visibly" (default discharge derived per target per UI.md §10 §7.2); not built

- T3 resolved: speculation is declared product meaning; Claude's closed four-value `impact` enum (`.reversible`·`.consequential`·`.destructive`·`.external`) REJECTED — the values overlap (a trade is external+consequential+irreversible; account deletion destructive but recoverable 30 days; a message external, retractable, showable as "sending" not "delivered"; a rename reversible but may notify externally) — repeating the `at` mistake; consensus: one required `impact:` word as an extensible PROFILE (`.routine`, `.reversible`, `.destructive`, `.external`) over a record of at least presentation-before-receipt, reversibility/compensation, external commitment, consequence/safeguard, receipt meaning; mixed cases via `with { … }`; omission refused; compiler derives ELIGIBILITY to speculate from reach/read sets, the impact policy grants PERMISSION — both required; ratify the record before the catalogue (T12). *[WEB L1416–1454]* — evidence: AUTHORITY doc holds it; grep `impact` in std-ui none
- T3 follow-on resolved: `.destructive` REQUIRES an explicit SAFEGUARD as a refusal for a production conformance profile (warnings too easy for machine authors to normalise away), not exactly confirm/undo; valid discharges: undo, staged deletion/trash, confirmation, re-authentication, a second approver, a typed phrase, a domain workflow making commitment explicit; generic confirmation often inferior to undo (habituation); undo window discharges only if restoration is real ("an undo over lossy compensation does not discharge it" — the rule most likely gamed); gallery generates traces; compiler proves wiring, user evidence judges comprehension; `safeguard: .none` only via typed waiver/mandate, visible in every receipt. *[WEB L1455–1477]* — evidence: UI.md §3.4 default discharge; not built
- T12 Claude's proposed impact profile table: `.routine` (shown done; reversal by any later ordinary action; no external; no safeguard); `.reversible` (shown done + undo affordance; compensating action within a window; undo); `.destructive` (pending; per safeguard; required); `.external` ("sending", never "done"; declared compensation, may be lossy; commits to another authority; declared); open question whether `.routine` vs `.reversible` is real. *[WEB L1746–1759]* — evidence: none
- T12 resolved: keep both `.routine` and `.reversible` — a user-facing GUARANTEE (`.reversible` promises a declared inverse/compensating action, a real window, an undo affordance); "shown done" REJECTED (impersonates uncommitted fact) → `.routine` = "speculative next state may render; no recovery promised; failure reconciles and is announced" (no badge required, inspector state `speculative` until receipt); `.reversible` = speculative with undo obligation, compensation records a new fact, never erases history; `.destructive` conservative base (no completed presentation before authoritative receipt, recovery named, every invocation discharges; trash may override presentation); `.external` distinguishes SERVER receipt ("durable work accepted" → "sending") from EXTERNAL receipt ("sent/paid/booked"); two-level rule: action declares recovery mechanism + minimum safeguard, each invocation (button, CLI, agent) discharges context-dependent safeguard — confirmation not proved because some page wraps the action in `ask`; four profiles are conservative ergonomic profiles, not exhaustive categories. *[WEB L1760–1798]* — evidence: AUTHORITY doc; nothing in code

### events (25)

#### events — PLANNED (3)

- Event turns: a handler is NOT atomic; compiler knows every pause and PRINTS THE CUTS; segment-commit reference default: segment begins at dispatch or resumption, ends at first pause/return/uncaught failure, its local writes commit atomically (one patch), resumption opens a new segment with a NEW read frame, failure/cancellation never rolls back a committed segment (only compensating write), an action has its own commit boundary, one intent across pauses = a draft carrying its base then one action; so "set pending; call; show result" is responsive by construction. *[WEB L405–423]* — evidence: ARCH "Failure is contained to its turn" (turns BUILT, tests/trapped_turn); segment printing not built; UI.md §0 keeps "segment-commit event turns"
- Gesture law (flagship temporal knowledge): `error: clipboard.write needs the click's gesture, and share_link pauses before it (line 3)` / `help: compute the link before the click, or clipboard.write_later(share_link(doc))`; `preventDefault` likewise decided before any pause. *[WEB L965–970]* — evidence: none found; docs/2026_10_18_EVENTS.md does not implement it (UNKNOWN for preventDefault handling in runtime/dom)
- Gap 3 Gestures (`.37`): input is click/change/input — no drag, pinch, fling, long-press; the gesture arena (composition) is the hard half; depends on payload widening avra-4a34, scheduler #223, source-backed state `.34.4`. DOM event table lists drag events; no gesture layer. *[DERIV L750–750]* — evidence: tree/dom_event.av

- Rule: `on` is events only; platform and capability are VALUES read by `if`: `if hovered { … }` · `if compact { … }` · `if platform is .ios { … }` (lowered to CSS/traits where possible). *[UI L703–703]* — evidence: `on` = declared events BUILT (ARCH seam 4, EVENTS ruling 2); hover via StateStyle data; `hovered`/`compact`/`platform` values absent
- Bar: interaction — focus, keyboard, pointer and gesture events; overlays with focus trapping and dismissal; forms with validation and error display; async with pending/failed/empty. *[LIBRARY L29–31]* — evidence: focus/keys (terminal, `key`), dialog dismiss, model_form errors, Async<T> BUILT; gestures absent
- T6 resolved: segment-commit is the reference default over holding all writes until turn end (which withholds feedback across a network wait); exact definition (begin at dispatch/resumption; end at first pause/return/uncaught failure; local writes atomic at boundary; new read frame on resumption; no rollback of committed segments; action has own commit boundary); "set pending; call; show result" responsive; cross-pause intent = draft + one action; compiler prints cuts in `explain`, warns pre-pause reads used after resumption, refuses rollback-implying syntax → §6.1. *[WEB L1539–1561]* — evidence: turns commit per handler run in std-ui loop (ARCH "Failure is contained to its turn"); cut printing / warnings not built

- DECIDED (owner 2026-09-29): before agents land autonomously, existing `LICENSED` comments are grandfathered as an approved baseline, and every new, widened or renewed one needs a mandate (AUTHORITY §14). *[WEB L1250–1253]* — evidence: `LICENSED I<n>` law in CLAUDE.md + tools/idioms.baseline; mandate mechanism not built
- T5 resolved: product-law waivers need owner, reason, expiry and appear in `explain` and receipts; Claude's proposal of a typed `@waive(law: .target_size, profile: .web_aa, reason: "…", owner: "…", expires: "2027-03")` and migrating `LICENSED` to it — reviewer: ONE logical mechanism, not one mandatory syntax (an annotation is no safer than a comment; an agent can synthesise either); real safety = REQUESTING and AUTHORIZING are separate powers; normalise both into one `Waiver` row (law, scope, target/profile, reason, accountable owner, issued time, expiry/review time, authorizing mandate/signature); `@waive(...)` is the Avra surface, structured `LICENSED` stays the bootstrap/foreign-source surface; build/deploy accepts only when the current mandate permits law/scope/owner, else adding `@waive` is itself a refusal ("the human writes the laws; the machine writes the bodies"); build the row + receipt first, teach keepers to project `LICENSED` into it, migrate spellings only where it removes machinery, don't churn the repo. → T13. *[WEB L1506–1537]* — evidence: `LICENSED I<n>` + tools/idioms.baseline exist; no Waiver row / @waive
- T13 Claude: separation of powers only holds if authorizing is outside the author's write reach (an agent editing source can edit a mandate file — same power, two names); options (a) SIGNED mandates (human key, compiler verifies), (b) VCS-anchored (protected paths + human review at landing: CODEOWNERS, `tools/land.sh`), (c) DEPLOY-anchored (mandate from deploy env); lean (a) for deploy receipts, (b) as dev floor; sub-question whether repo `LICENSED` comments (agent-writable today) should get the same split. *[WEB L1800–1815]* — evidence: none
- T13 reviewer: (a)(b)(c) are a CHAIN not alternatives; ROOT OF TRUST outside agent write reach (org public key/policy service pinned by deploy env or trusted compiler installation; a key in the mutable tree is not an anchor); source `@waive` is only a REQUEST; human review / protected workflow / policy service issues a SIGNED mandate scoped to project, law, waiver id, target/environment, owner, policy digest, expiry, parameters/budget. *[WEB L1816–1825]* — evidence: none
- T13 chain cont.: the signed artifact may live beside source for reproducibility (agent can copy/delete, not alter scope); VCS protection is an issuance workflow not proof (`CODEOWNERS` in an agent-writable tree and a local `land.sh` are not trust boundaries; CI can turn protected-review evidence into a signed attestation — that is); deploy environment verifies against its own trust root and current revocation/policy epoch, deploy receipt records mandate digest and every waiver discharged, production never trusts "the build said it was authorized"; local dev: an unendorsed waiver yields an explicitly NON-CONFORMING preview/build, never a release/deploy receipt; `avra check` refusing vs reporting `UnendorsedWaiver` is a profile choice, red status impossible to mistake for passage. *[WEB L1825–1840]* — evidence: none
- T13 resolved: repo `LICENSED` comments acceptable TODAY only because human landing review is the real authority — not adequate for autonomous landing (an agent could license away the ratchet it is judged by); once the common row exists, grandfather existing licenses as approved baseline and require a mandate for every new/widened/renewed one; migrate the enforcement split, not necessarily the syntax; consensus: signed mandates for release/deploy, protected VCS or policy service as issuance paths, deploy-side verification as final boundary, visibly non-conforming local previews; Claude concedes `tools/land.sh` is no anchor; `LICENSED` half raised to the owner as §23 item 0b. *[WEB L1841–1858]* — evidence: owner decided 0b (L1250); mandate machinery not built
- Law 15: a font is licensed or refused — licence read from the font's `name` table; a face with none must be told one; embedding bits (`fsType`) read; a restricted face refused where it would be embedded. *[ASSETS L80–80]* — evidence: grep fsType empty

#### security — PARTIAL (1)

- Security checklist: contextual output escaping (HTML, attributes, URLs, CSS, scripts) with types distinguishing plain text, trusted markup, safe URL classes, style values, unsafe conversion visible; trusted markup/sanitization and DOM sinks; CSRF + SameSite; clickjacking/embedding, origin/CORS, `postMessage` target validation; open redirects, dangerous URL schemes; session fixation, token storage, credential leakage; snapshot/source-map/error-report redaction; third-party script/supply-chain integrity; SW scope/update compromise; uploads (type, size, scanning, decompression limits); rate/enumeration/abuse budgets; retention/export/deletion/regional; capability revocation + audit evidence (receipts). *[WEB L1017–1034]* — evidence: contextual escaping BUILT (std-ui/src/realize/html/html.av "Every value is escaped by the writer for the context it lands in"); safe URL type via sealed `Url` (CLAUDE.md sealed law, std-ui/src/tree/url.av); rest not built

#### security — BUILT (5)

- Law 6: a standing is never handed to another component; one kept by a component and reached by another is not read — state can be lost to a moved place, never read by a stranger. *[ARCH L465–467]* — evidence: tree/standing.av
- A URL IN THE TREE IS CHECKED: `href`/`src` carry `Url` (`tree/url.av`), a SEALED type with NO unsafe value — only that module builds one (a site-internal reference or one of `Scheme`'s); `url(text)` reads foreign text strictly and answers absence for a foreign scheme (read from the first path segment), a network path (`//host`, slashes or backslashes), any control character; `local("/docs")`, `https("x.dev")`, `absolute(scheme, after)` build from program text and write it safe (behind `./`, behind `/.`, control chars percent-escaped); no target checks a URL. *[ARCH L718–726]* — evidence: std-ui/src/tree/url.av:77 `fn url`, :90 `fn local`, :104 `fn absolute`
- Law 3: no asset is found by text; a handle exists only by naming a declaration or member, no string lookup, so what a program reaches is EXACTLY what it can show. *[ASSETS L68–68]* — evidence: assets are consts; Picture sealed
- Law 5: a path is a literal from the declaring package's root and stays inside it — no `..` out, no absolute path, no symlink out. *[ASSETS L70–70]* — evidence: features/fns/embed_voices.av ("`embed` reads inside its own package", "`embed` follows no link")
- Defect PROBED: `embed("../../../../../../../../../etc/hosts")` and `embed("/etc/hosts")` compiled (answering 256) — any package's const could bake a build-machine file into a binary; law 5 holds for `embed` too. Fixed: embed refuses outside-package paths and symlinks. *[ASSETS L254–256]* — evidence: features/fns/embed_voices.av "`embed` reads inside its own package"

#### security — REJECTED (1)

- Rejected: `data:` URI placeholders — widens CSP and the `Url` type; a CSS gradient does it in 40 bytes. *[ASSETS L249–249]* — evidence: doc

#### security — UNKNOWN (1)

- Law 14: a vector is parsed into a model with no unsafe shape — no script, external reference, event attribute or `<style>`; targets write from the model, none sanitises. SVG is recognized as Picture; a safe vector model not confirmed. *[ASSETS L79–79]* — evidence: std-assets header "PNG, JPEG or SVG"

#### vocabulary — PARTIAL (4)

- The TEN words: `app` (root: name, icon, screens, targets, reach), `screen` (navigable destination, signature = route), `view` (reusable piece that may own state), `state` (a place; in a body owned here, in a param owned by caller), `at` (state policy profile), `@model` (data with identity, the ORM), `@action` (only door to authoritative change), `draft` (branch of truth knowing its base), `task` (async work owned by a view's life), `animate` (a write carrying its transition). *[UI L189–200]* — evidence: `state` BUILT (seat_builder.av SeatMark.state, is_state_stmt); `@model` std-db; `@action` std-action; task-owned-by-control BUILT as ARCH seam 7 (work belongs to the control that asked); `app`/`screen`/`at`/`draft`/`animate` absent; `view` → component composition (ARCH)
- `page` retires into `screen` (one word across targets); `State<T>` retires into `state` parameters. *[UI L202–205]* — evidence: no `State<T>`; `screen` not built
- Nouns: view (pure fn from reads to semantic tree); semantic node (interaction contract role/name/value/state/keyboard/focus, realized as native HTML, platform control, or canvas+peer); cell (state value with identity and policy); read (observation of a cell path, query, or source); write (path write to a cell within an event turn); event turn (work an event starts, cut at pause points); pause (`MayPause`, inferred, inspectable); draft (branch of some truth knowing its base); action (only door to authoritative state); receipt (durable record of a committed action). *[WEB L125–136]* — evidence: view/node/state BUILT (std-ui/src/tree/view.av, node.av; `state` cells features/places.av); MayPause/draft/receipt not in tree (grep empty)
- Three identifiers, never aliases: semantic identity (state lifetime + reconciliation); origin (`OriginId` + `OriginChain`: generated expansion → component declaration → invocation role/call site); runtime node id (correlates observed browser node with build artifacts). *[WEB L143–149]* — evidence: semantic identity BUILT as sites (std-ui/src/tree/identity.av, sites.av; COMPILED "Identity: sites, not ordinals"); OriginId not in tree; node→source jump planned DERIV §L

#### vocabulary — BUILT (2)

- A model learns few words; `list`, `button`, `toolbar`, `form` are ordinary imported components found by `use`, documented by `///`, never reserved. *[UI L183–187]* — evidence: std-ui/components/*.av `export component`; ARCH "a module's own component word is reserved nowhere"

#### agents/mcp — PLANNED (8)

- A derived door reaches agents only when listed (`tools: [Card.update]`) — agents act through named doors (AUTONOMOUS §4.1). *[UI L734–735]* — evidence: std-mcp tools are hand-listed; no derivation from models
- `screen ask(b, state prompt: string = "") { field(prompt, "Ask about ${b.name}"); answer(helper, prompt, about: b) /* a dyn View from the app's own views, checked whole */ }` — generative UI. *[TOUR L209–213]* — evidence: none found
- Gap: `answer(...)` in render is an agent call — a pause and a cost; needs a cache/budget rule. *[TOUR L361–361]* — evidence: open
- Linked projections, not one tree: one semantic source projects consumer-specific contracts linked by identity — a11y (platform semantics), tests (stable queries), agents (typed intent, side effects, authority, confirmation, result schemas); agents' AUTHORITY surface is actions never the screen tree (visual refactors must not change automation contracts; page content must not steer privileged tools). *[WEB L696–702]* — evidence: UI.md §7 "Agents are users"; nothing built for agents in std-ui
- Agent approval derived from auth + impact + delegation mandate + live state (AUTHORITY §11); agents discharge safeguards differently from a button, same obligation; Generative UI: an agent composes a `dyn View` from the app's catalogue, catalogue items declare composition constraints, a11y semantics, disclosure, action authority and contexts, compiler validates the WHOLE composition. *[WEB L703–709]* — evidence: `dyn View` exists as a type (std-ui/src/tree/view.av) but no agent catalogue
- T14 resolved: one SAFEGUARD OBLIGATION, multiple context-specific discharges; `auth` = may the principal request; `impact` = consequences, receipt meaning, recovery, MINIMUM safeguard; a mandate = what this agent may do unattended under which predicates/budgets/time window; live state may still refuse; formula `required safeguard = impact minimum + live context − mandate discharge`; human confirmation and agent approval are different discharges; defaults `.routine` no approval, `.destructive` approval, `.external` required until narrower rule proved; action parameters matter (refund $5 vs $50,000); auth sensitivity/disclosure may require approval even for routine impact; source may declare `automation: .eligible` or request a narrower safeguard, cannot authorize `unattended` — Claude's `with { agent: .unattended }` REJECTED as self-approval; grant in signed mandate (e.g. "support agent may call `refund` unattended when amount ≤ $50 and daily total ≤ $500"); weakening the minimum safeguard is a separately named waiver; receipt records impact version, live authorization witness, mandate id/budget consumption, approval/safeguard discharge, predicate inputs, authoritative/external result. *[WEB L1860–1906]* — evidence: AUTHORITY doc; UI.md §7 "Agents are users"; none built
- AI sketch: `agent support { model: "claude-sonnet-5"  instructions: "…"  tool find_order  tool refund { approval: required } }`; `train sentiment { data: reviews  epochs: 10  on epoch { log(self.loss) } }`. (std-mcp exists but no `agent` component.) *[EXPANSION L483–493]* — evidence: no `component agent|train`
