### native (9)

#### native — PLANNED (7)

- Why UIKit and Android Views, not SwiftUI/Compose: both reachable from compiled Avra through the C ABI (Obj-C runtime, JNI), accessible, mature, and neither runs a second diffing framework under ours; SwiftUI/Compose are `foreign` islands where only they offer a feature. *[UI L472–476]* — evidence: no native realizer
- Honest exceptions: WidgetKit and Live Activities accept only SwiftUI, Android widgets use Glance/RemoteViews; glances are restricted enough to translate, so those targets GENERATE Swift/Kotlin as a projection needing `swiftc`/`kotlinc` — printed, never hidden. *[UI L477–480]* — evidence: none found
- Decision 3: native realizers are UIKit / Android Views through the C ABI; SwiftUI/Compose are islands (recommend yes). *[UI L662–663]* — evidence: no native realizer
- Gap 4 App lifecycle (`.38`): only `avra_start`; no background/resume/terminate; depends on routing `.34.1`, wasm entry. *[DERIV L751–751]* — evidence: none
- iOS/macOS: asset catalog 1x/2x/3x, vectors kept, dark and locale as catalog variants; template images, `@std/icons` names use SF Symbols, RTL flag; fonts as bundle files + plist rows, sizes via Dynamic Type. *[ASSETS L109–109]* — evidence: none
- Android: `res/drawable-*dpi/*.webp`, `-night`, `-ldrtl`, locale qualifiers; VectorDrawable XML from the model with `autoMirrored`; `res/font/`. *[ASSETS L110–110]* — evidence: none
- Drawn (canvas, GPU): drawn sizes in a trivially decoded format; icons as path data filled by the target; the font file + that target's shaper (§6). *[ASSETS L111–111]* — evidence: none

#### native — PARTIAL (1)

- Gap 5 Platform abstraction (`.39`): density, safe areas, native input methods, per-platform a11y decide whether cross-platform is true; depends on native targets L6 (`.29`), ambient avra-xubk.6.2. `@host(web:…, terminal:…)` per-platform event validation built. *[DERIV L752–752]* — evidence: components/button.av `@host(...)`

#### native — BUILT (1)

- A native target (`native/sources.av`) loops over the inbox: `later`/`repeating` leave a job, the loop spawns it, it parks and posts, posted work runs as a turn; the loop block owns its tasks. WORK BELONGS TO THE CONTROL THAT ASKED (its path): after a paint, a worker whose control left is cancelled and its inbox leftovers dropped unrun; the terminal's stdin is such a source. *[ARCH L140–147]* — evidence: std-ui/src/native/sources.av; tests/loop_owner*

### wasm (7)

#### wasm — PARTIAL (2)

- WASM yes for substantial logic; JS fragments compete for small islands (§11.3). *[WEB L115–116]* — evidence: wasm page target built (docs/2026_10_02_WASM32.md, runtime/dom/bootstrap.js); JS-fragment islands not built
- WASM reaches DOM through the JS host boundary; DOM applier size is a BUDGET HYPOTHESIS, total cost includes loader, ABI, string marshaling, event delegation, scheduling, serialization, error recovery, polyfills — measure it; two strategies compete per island: tiny JS fragments (restricted fragment like BEND's kernel fragment) and WASM for substantial logic. *[WEB L821–828]* — evidence: wasm + JS host built (runtime/dom/bootstrap.js, page.html; docs/2026_10_02_WASM32.md); JS-fragment strategy not built

#### wasm — BUILT (4)

- Failure: a WASM trap terminates its instance; traps CONTAINED where instance and checkpoint boundaries make recovery possible; define the isolation model before promising more. *[WEB L864–867]* — evidence: ARCH "Failure is contained to its turn"; std-ui/src/tests/trapped_turn, contained*
- A target WITHOUT TASKS (the page) realizes the seam from host callbacks: `setTimeout` or a `fetch` answer re-enters via an exported fn that posts the `Act` and runs one turn, as `avra_ui_event` does; the headless screen runs the native loop under a virtual clock (`screen.running`, `screen.advance`); a target is what the door paints on; control text crosses in a seat sized for exactly its octets (no longest length). *[ARCH L148–156]* — evidence: app/wants.av (virtual clock); tests/observed_reask `screen.advance(ms(10))`
- No JIT: the page runs a real wasm module (the shipping artifact); a change rebuilds via `avra build --target wasm --wasm_reactor` as a child; the store makes it incremental (moved files read again, their objects remade, one link, one `wasm-opt`). Board under load: cold 2.7 s, one-line edit 1.1–1.6 s, unchanged 0.2–0.3 s; warm edit compiler phases ~0.4 s (link 100 ms largest), rest `wasm-opt -Oz` and two process starts. *[ARCH L632–640]* — evidence: dev.av:441 `["build", "--target", …, "--wasm_reactor", …]`
- The toolchain is found: `--target wasm` is whichever WASI triple this machine's clang has a libc for — `CC` when set, else `LLVM_PREFIX`'s clang, then PATH's; a machine with none hears which compilers were asked for which triples. *[ARCH L693–696]* — evidence: commands/dev.av `self.tools.target`

#### wasm — OVERRIDDEN (1)

- Fibers in the browser: generated state machines at pause points (broad support, deterministic code size, cancellation, debugging, differential oracle); JSPI optional backend; JSPI ships Chrome 137, Firefox 139, Safari 27.0 (2026-09-17), read 2026-09-29; shipped-in-all-three ≠ Baseline Widely Available, support floor decides. *[WEB L829–835]* — evidence: docs/2026_10_18_WASM_TASKS.md "One park contract; the host supplies the suspension" — host-supplied park (JSPI, runtime/dom/tasks.js) chosen, not generated state machines

### tui (5)

#### tui — PARTIAL (1)

- Terminal: `[image: alt]`; pixels only under a graphics capability, opt-in; icon as a cell character else the name; no fonts. *[ASSETS L108–108]* — evidence: realize/tui (UNKNOWN exact rendering)

#### tui — BUILT (3)

- Seam 9, A SECOND TARGET FIRES EVENTS: terminal app is `mount(view)` (`terminal/terminal.av`): tree drawn as lines in a width, ONE focused control, keys through the same door — Tab/Shift-Tab move focus among speaking controls, Enter presses or changes over, typed character fills a field, Ctrl-D ends; reading-order controls one definition shared with headless (`tree/controls.av`); focus kept by path (follows a keyed row); a field shows what the view says. *[ARCH L163–172]* — evidence: std-ui/src/terminal/terminal.av; tree/controls.av
- The board (`tools/ui-board`) is one view with two entries: `web/` mounts on a page, `term/` in a terminal. *[ARCH L179–180]* — evidence: tools/ui-board
- Other targets: headless screen is the tree (holds nothing unnamed); terminal keeps one thing — focus by path (follows a keyed row); keeps no text, so a field shows what the view says and one without `value` shows none (avra-8sb5.11.336). *[ARCH L371–375]* — evidence: terminal/terminal.av; tree/controls.av

#### tui — OVERRIDDEN (1)

- Input read as octets: a non-character octet dropped alone, a cut char/escape finished by the next read. A terminal in usual (cooked) mode sends whole lines, so focus can't move without acting and text needs Enter; keys one at a time need raw mode, a row the runtime lacks (avra-8sb5.11.279). Raw mode now exists. *[ARCH L172–179]* — evidence: terminal/terminal.av:34 `extern fn avra_fd_tty_raw`, :368 "full raw mode"

### UI targets and the live wire

> 2026-10-02. Records the layering that makes the UI neutral, how the
> live target's wire works, and its KNOWN cost. The DOM target is the
> first projection of a neutral model, not the model.

#### What is HTML-only (the thin part)

- the tag names (`span`/`div`/`h1`) and the writer that spells them;
- classes and the atomic stylesheet (`realize/dom/frame.av`'s `sheet`),
  CSS variables — **there is no CSS on Apple**;
- the JS bootstrap and the op *text* encoding — both exist only because
  the browser has no differ.

#### What is target-neutral (the substance)

- the vocabulary: `Prim`, `Attribute`, `Box`, `Style`, `Layout`, `Theme`, `Name`;
- the eager, keyed `Node` tree;
- the reconciliation semantics: keyed identity, order, insert/move/remove,
  attribute/text set;
- themes and tokens, interaction states, accessible names.

#### Native projection (Swift / iOS / macOS)

| neutral | SwiftUI / UIKit |
|---|---|
| `Prim.Column` / `.Row` / `.Surface` | `VStack` / `HStack` / container + background |
| `.Text` / `.Heading` | `Text` + `.font(.headline/.title…)` |
| `.Button` / `.Link` | `Button` / `Link` (or `NavigationLink`) |
| `.List` / `.Item` | `List` + `ForEach` |
| `.Switch` / `.Checkbox` | `Toggle` |
| `.Field` / `.Slider` / `.Progress` | `TextField` / `Slider` / `ProgressView` |
| `.Dialog` / overlay | `.sheet` / `.alert` |
| `Box.style` (tone/fill/radius/face) | `.foregroundStyle` / `.background` / `clipShape` / `.font` |
| `Box.layout` (axis/gap/pad/align/distribute) | `spacing`, `.padding`, `alignment`, `Spacer()` |
| `Style.states` (hover/focus/pressed) | `ButtonStyle`, `.hoverEffect`, `@FocusState` |
| `Theme` tokens | a Swift struct + `Color`/`Font`; contrast → `\.colorSchemeContrast` |
| `Attribute.Label/Name/Text`, `Urgency` | `.accessibilityLabel`, `AccessibilityNotification` |
| `Node.key` | structural identity — `ForEach(id:)` wants exactly this |

##### The design choice for native

1. SHIP OPS — a Swift applier analogous to the JS bootstrap. Works, but
   re-implements diffing.
2. SHIP THE TREE — a Swift `Node` model plus a `NodeView` mapping `Prim`
   → views, and let **SwiftUI diff it** (the platform IS the differ).

**Take (2) on Apple and Android.** The op stream exists for targets with
NO differ (DOM, canvas, print); a target with one consumes the `Node`
value and diffs natively. Both consume one `Node`, one vocabulary, one
theme. Web pay: HTML; Apple pay: SwiftUI emission + a small runtime.

#### The live wire (DOM) and its cost

`avra_dom_frame(ptr, len)` — ONE host call per render, a frame the
`bootstrap.js` reconciles by key. Cheap today: one call, O(nodes) Map
lookups, elements REUSED (identity survives).

KNOWN COST, in order:

1. THE TEXT ENCODING IS THE BRITTLE PART. Value escaping is mirrored on
   two sides (`esc` in Avra, `unesc` in JS) — a drift is silent, and this
   is the "a guard and the thing it guards must read the same bytes"
   hazard. JS `split`/`indexOf` per line also allocates per op.
2. AVRA STRINGS ARE IMMUTABLE, so building the frame concats: a
   `"C ${path} ${tag}"` per node is a small allocation per op. A 10k-node
   tree makes ~30k strings + lists per render.
3. FULL RE-RENDER BY CHOICE (M1): every change re-emits the whole tree.
4. THE EAGER TREE IS REBUILT per render — O(tree) program allocations,
   independent of the bridge.

##### The fixes, in value order

1. **BINARY FRAME, not text.** Flat fixed-width records `[op, key, arg,
   value_ref]` in linear memory, with an interned string table. No
   escaping (kills the drift), no parsing, no per-op string allocation.
   The encoding changes behind the SAME row — vocabulary, tree, theme,
   keys and reconciler are untouched.
2. **STATIC NODES EMITTED ONCE.** Most of a UI never changes: emit a
   subtree as static, reference it by key, send only dynamic nodes. Turns
   a keystroke from O(tree) to O(changed). Needs the read set (M2).
3. **A REVISION / NO-CHANGE FRAME.** An unchanged render costs one int.
4. **A REUSABLE SCRATCH BUFFER.** Grow-once linear memory; no per-frame
   allocation.
5. **A PROTOCOL VERSION IN THE HEADER**, so a stale bootstrap REFUSES
   rather than silently misapplying.
6. **KEEP THE TEXT AS A DEBUG PROJECTION** (P7: inspectable) — one
   function that renders a frame as lines, used by tests and `explain`,
   never on the wire.

## Appendix B — the emit probe (2026-10-04, recovered from `4db054539e`)

A dated measurement; never landed.

### UI emit probe — where a paint actually spends its work

> 2026-10-04. Before a consumer is built for `lowering_plan`
> (`features/read_set.av`), four probes, run against the current
> compiler. The result redirects the epic and fixes a defect it would
> otherwise have shipped.

#### P3 — the frame, not construction, is the cost of a paint

`tools/bench/uibench` builds a 40-leaf `Node` tree and times the
paint's stages natively (200 runs, 82 ops, a 1331-byte frame):

| stage | ns/run |
|---|---|
| `construct` (`branch(40)`) | 19,900 |
| `plan(node)` (walk the tree into ops) | 10,600 |
| `frame(node)` (`plan` + serialize every op) | 48,900 |
| `Live.diff` (frame + byte compare, unchanged) | 50,600 |

The component layer makes construction heavier — a 40-row
`view().describe()` measured ~62µs in a scratch probe against a
~251µs frame — but does not change the conclusion. `frame` dominates,
by 2.5x over bare `Node` construction and by 4.6x over the walk alone:
the **serialize** is the cost.

What `Live.diff` does today with an unchanged subtree: `frame(root)`
walks the WHOLE tree, serializes every op into a fresh `Bytes`, then
compares that to the last frame and returns `no_change` — two bytes.
`no_change` is a whole-frame equality, never a per-subtree skip. So
`Hoist`'s value is not only "build once": it is also **never
re-planning or re-serializing a subtree that cannot have changed**, and
that is the larger half.

#### P1 — a Hoist op stream is constant, but the plan called OPEN sites Hoist

`frame(branch(40))` twice is byte-identical (1331 bytes), so a
state-free, input-free subtree is genuinely constant.

But `fn f(x: int) -> int { x + 1 }` read no state and held no call —
and the plan said **Hoist**. Its value differs on every call. The plan
proved "reads no state", not "constant"; `hoistable_of`/`site_lowering_of`
let parameters, receivers, and outer locals through. The `Direct` leaf
had the identical hole (`fn f(x: int) -> int { state n: int = 0; n + x }`
was `Direct(n)`, a targeted write that would miss `x`). A consumer that
took `Hoist` at its word would have frozen any parameterized view or
screen (`screen board(id: TaskId)`) to its first call's arguments.

Fixed in `read_set.av`: a site must be **closed** (`closed_of` — no
parameter, receiver, or binding from outside the site; a `state` place
is the read set's, not this check's) before it earns `Hoist` or
`Direct`. Four spec cases pin it; the package's 7760 cases pass.

#### P2 — construction cannot be intercepted today (the crux)

A component instance lowers to its fields array, then to a `dyn View`
box `[fields, describe_fn, type_tag]`. From `avra ir` of `heading
"Tasks" { rank: 1 }`:

    r7  = avra_array_sized(6)         // fields
    r8  = avra_array_new()
    call avra_array_push_moved(r8, r7)
    r9  = addr @std.ui.components.heading.describe
    call avra_array_push(r8, r9)      // the vtable's one method
    r10 = int 490752735               // the dyn TYPE tag
    call avra_array_push(r8, r10)

The `.describe()` that turns a component into a `Node` is a `dyn`
dispatch whose subject comes from `List<dyn View>` — type-erased at
the `items` boundary (`realize/view.av` generates `[v.describe() for v
in self.items]`). The compiler's `lowering_plan` is over the VIEW
expression sites, and `site_lowering_of` never sees the `describe()`
consumer. There is no seam at which the compiler can replace a
component construction with a prebuilt `Node` without **owning view
construction** — devirtualizing `List<dyn View>` and its `describe`
chain. Shape (a) as literally stated is not available today. That is
the epic's deepest tension, named.

#### P4 — a construction site carries a per-TYPE tag, not a per-site id

The only constant at a construction site is the `dyn` type tag
(490752735 for `heading`, 210102596 for `text`) — identical for two
`heading`s at different source sites. `Action.site` carries a
content-addressed site for EVENTS only. A per-site identity on the
node the frame walks does not exist; it would be new.

#### What follows

The measurement redirects the first slice: the win is not only
avoiding construction, it is avoiding the whole-tree plan+serialize on
every paint. That needs the frame layer to skip an unchanged subtree,
which needs a per-site identity on the `Node` (or the component value).
P4 says it is new; P2 says the compiler cannot inject it without owning
view construction. The least-risky first move is to stamp the
content-addressed site the plan ALREADY computes
(`SitePlan.site = expr_fingerprint`) onto the component value at
construction, carry it to the `Node`, and cache the subtree's op
stream/bytes by site in `frame.av` — with the P1 fix making `Hoist`
trustworthy for that consumer.

## Appendix C — wasm tasks: three paragraphs that never reached `docs/2026_10_18_WASM_TASKS.md`

From `e9464d69d` and `52f880516d`.

## The shadow-stack guard: measured, and not shipped as a per-call check

A check at every fn's entry (read the stack pointer, compare with the running
task's floor, call the overflow) was built through the backend and measured
at +72% on fib 40 (231 to 397 ms, wasm32 -O2 via binaryen 133). Its case
cannot occur from Avra source: a call's depth is bounded first by the
engine's own stack (a `RangeError` the host speaks as the overflow), and the
only frames that sit in linear memory are a few spills no Avra recursion
accumulates. So it was removed, and the guard is the cheap form native also
uses beyond its guarded stacks: a CANARY at the foot of each task's block,
checked at every handover, so an overrun is caught at the task's next
switch. Building it found a real defect on the way: the thread's first stack
was never marked as started, so a handover back to it started it as a fresh
task and gave it a stack of its own.

10. `task_recursion_depth` (landed as the `stack` and `stack_overflow` fixtures):
    recursion 5,000 deep inside a task succeeds under all three engines
    (10,000 does not: the native task stack of 256 KiB overflows first, so
    the claim is made at what every engine can run). Recursion past any
    stack ends native and wasm with status 2 and the words `a task's stack
    overflowed`; the evaluator refuses in its own words (its 100,000-call
    limit). On wasm the limit that bites first is the ENGINE's call stack,
    a bare `RangeError`, so the host speaks it as the same overflow
    (`faultWords`); a break that drops that mapping turns the fixture red.

- ENTRIES ARE SERIAL (decided): an event that arrives while an entry is parked
  waits its turn (`tasks.enter` chains them), because turns are serial and two
  entries would otherwise both stand in for the program's one main stack.
  Witnessed by `tasks_test.js` (three together answer, never more than one inside).
